市場焦點
美國
• 9月CPI年增3.7%、PPI年增6.3%，雙雙高於前期 ，能源價格的急升是通膨回升的主要推力 。
雖然核心CPI年增率僅升至2.6%，但住房與服務價格仍維持黏著，使通膨降溫過程出現反覆；
同時PPI增幅明顯高於 CPI，反映企業成本壓力偏高 ，未來不排除進一步向終端價格傳導 。整
體而言，CPI與PPI同步回升反映通膨降溫進程仍存在不確定性 ，進而令市場重估通膨回落速
度與對Fed貨幣政策路徑的審慎預期。
AI
• AI雲端業者 CoreWeave的5年期CDS利差一度突破 1,140個基點 ，旗下兩檔債券殖利率逼近
15%，相關賣壓並進一步蔓延至股票市場 。全球最大半導體設備商ASML表示，客戶已開始洽
談2029至2030年的訂單；晶圓代工龍頭台積電財報亦優於市場預期 。與此同時，台灣出口增

速創3月以來新高，韓國9月半導體出口年增逾260%，顯示半導體需求持續強勁，預期第三季
相關企業獲利仍有望維持強勁。
中國
• 9月通膨加速主要源於中東能源衝擊及 AI硬體需求，能源和科技領域之外 ，普遍性的通縮壓力
仍在，彭博表示以GDP平減指數衡量 ，經歷三年下滑後 ，整體經濟預計將在 2026年走出通縮 ，
投資上短期可聚焦，受惠AI資本支出與能源價格上行的半導體、伺服器及能源相關族群。

美股獲利引擎轉向半導體/核心AI與能源
• 雖然標普500指數在2026年第三季展現出獲利韌性 ，但隨著油價上漲侵蝕利潤率 、消費者受通膨壓抑 ，以及分析師下調未
來預測動能，市場未來的容錯空間正在縮小。
• AI與半導體仍為獲利領頭羊：科技與半導體持續為標普500指數獲利成長的核心動力，其中約20家企業組成的核心AI一籃子
股票(權重約34.6%)對指數獲利的貢獻度已超過科技七雄(Mag 7)，且此優勢預計將至少延續至2028年底。
• 油價推升能源產業，接棒成為強棒：受到近期國際油價上揚影響，能源產業獲利預測顯著上修，預計獲利成長勁道將延續至
2027年初。在接下來的三個季度中，能源板塊的收益成長表現預計將超越科技七雄。
• 消費與運輸業面臨成本壓頂：相較於科技與能源的強勁，必需消費品與交通運輸業正承受顯著壓力。例如，達美航空受高燃

料成本拖累，而Nike與百事可樂則反映出消費者因持續通膨而縮減支出的困境。
7月以來EPS和營收成長預期修正
資料來源：Bloomberg，2026.10.16
剔除能源、科技七雄和AI一籃子股票後的指數收益成長

顧
利潤率高檔盤整、關稅退款帶來短補，但動能已見放緩
• 利潤率高檔盤整，淨利潤率提升受限：標普500成分股第二季營運利潤率(18.7%)與淨利潤率(15.6%)均創下新高。雖然第三
季營運利潤率預估可進一步提升至19.4%，但淨利潤率預計微幅上升至15.8%，顯示整體企業淨利潤的擴張動能正漸趨平緩 。
• 700億美元關稅退款提供短線補藥： 受惠於美國政府依據國際緊急經濟權力法 (IEEPA)的追償退稅，估計仍有至少700億美元
的關稅款將退還給標普 500成分股(主要集中於工業、非必需消費及醫療保健等 )，這預計能為2026年標普500指數每股收益
(EPS)成長帶來約235個基點、利潤率提升約34個基點的短期提振。
• 上修動能開始轉弱：雖然整體財報指引動量仍維持正值，但過去六週內，分析師對未來12個月EPS與收入的修正動能出現頻

繁下修，這意味著市場先前的極度樂觀情緒正在消退，未來財報季度要優於預期的門檻正在提高。
• 小結與投資建議： 標普500指數短期內靠著半導體 /AI與能源雙引擎拉抬 ，加上關稅退款的一次性利多注入 ，下半年整體獲
利依然具備支撐。然而，高油價帶來的營運成本上升 ，以及終端消費力道的疲軟 ，正逐漸侵蝕非科技/非能源企業的利潤 ，
而大盤廣度不足(僅少數板塊支撐獲利)，代表未來投資風險正逐漸集中 ，但AI算力需求仍屬硬性需求，但波動加劇下，投資
建議以定期定額方式投資美股核心AI與半導體龍頭股票。
利潤率預測
資料來源：Bloomberg，2026.10.16
各產業第二季度收到的關稅退款
未來12月EPS預期修正動能與營收

顧
美國9月CPI、PPI 同步升溫，能源衝擊擴大通膨與利率風險
• 能源價格急升，消費通膨再升溫：美國9月CPI月增0.6%、
年增3.7%，高於前月 3.4%，其中汽油月增 9.1%、能源
價格月增 5.2%，汽油單項即貢獻整體 CPI當月漲幅逾半；
另一方面，核心CPI月增0.3%、年增2.6%、住房指數月
增0.4%，顯示能源雖是本次通膨回升主因 ，但住房及部
分服務價格仍具黏著性，令通膨降溫進程面臨反覆。
• 上游價格大幅攀升 ，成本傳導風險升高： 9月PPI最終需
求月增1.3%、年增6.3%，創2022年3月以來最大增幅，
其中最終需求商品月增 2.6%，能源價格大漲 10.8%，汽
油價格更跳升 20.3%；核心PPI月增0.5%、年增5.0%，
顯示價格壓力不僅來自能源 ，也涉及部分商品與服務環

節；後續是否進一步推升至 CPI，仍須觀察企業成本轉
嫁程度及需求變化。
• 市場看法： 通膨回升恐提高 Fed再度升息機率 ，並延長
高利率維持時間 ，甚至不排除進一步緊縮的可能 ，短期
有利支撐美債殖利率及美元表現 ，不過股、債市後續走
勢仍將受到貨幣政策預期與風險情緒變化影響 。資產配
置建議可關注具成本轉嫁能力 、現金流穩健的能源類股；
債券則宜以短中天期 、高信用評等標的為主 ，以降低利
率敏感度。黃金可作為分散地緣政治及通膨風險的輔助
配置，但不宜將任何單一資產視為必然受惠標的。
CPI 與核心 CPI 走勢變動

顧
供給面推動中國9月CPI升0.9%
• 供給面推動中國9月通膨復甦：中國9月通膨數據顯示，CPI年增率由8月的0.8%升至0.9%，連續兩個月擴大，但低於市場預期
的1.0%，物價上漲主要受到能源及旅遊價格帶動，而食品價格跌幅進一步擴大，使整體通膨壓力仍相對溫和，扣除食品與能源
後，核心CPI維持在1.0%，反映內需需求尚未明顯升溫 。另一方面，PPI年增率由3.8%大幅升至5.0%，優於市場預期，且月增
率達1.2%，創4月以來最大漲幅，顯示上游原物料及工業品價格回升，須關注未來是否逐步傳導至消費端價格。
• 展望後市： 9月通膨加速主要源於中東能源衝擊及 AI硬體需求 ，能源和科技領域之外 ，普遍性的通縮壓力仍 在，彭博表示 以
GDP平減指數衡量，經歷三年下滑後，整體經濟預計將在2026年走出通縮，值得注意的是，這種供給面驅動的通膨復甦，將降

低官方推出需求刺激措施的緊迫感 ，而要反轉通縮循環正需要官方舉措，投資上短期可聚焦受惠AI資本支出與能源價格上行的
半導體、伺服器及能源相關族群。
資料來源：彭博，2026.10.14
通膨集中於能源和AI相關行業

顧
過去18個月來，新興市場股票表現亮眼，累積報酬率超過標普500指數
的兩倍
• 過去18個月來，新興市場股票表現亮眼，累積報酬率超過標普500指數的兩倍：主因為獲利帶動成長，整體而言，新興市場企
業獲利表現強勁 ，而且仍持續加速成長 ，高盛預估2026年新興市場企業盈餘成長率將達 55%，超過標普500企業盈餘成長速
度的兩倍，且新興市場股票估值較美國股市存在超過40%的折價空間，評價相對具吸引力，今年以來流入新興市場股市的資金
已接近500億美元，創下過去20年來最強勁紀錄之一。更重要的是新興市場國家目前貢獻了全球近 80%的AI硬體出口。隨著AI
產業持續發展，受惠範圍有望從少數龍頭企業擴大至更多國家及公司，特別是那些能夠協助解決供應鏈瓶頸的企業。
• 結論：後市來看在AI產業趨勢、相對低估的評價水準，以及強勁企業獲利成長三大因素支撐下，新興市場股票未來一年仍具備

進一步上漲的潛力，有機會延續目前的強勢表現。

AI投資支撐美國成長，融資需求升高使長端利率與信用風險並存
• AI基礎建設持續擴張 ，成為企業投資重要動能 ：大型雲端業者
持續投入資料中心 、半導體、網路設備與電力基礎設施 ，帶動
相關產業資本支出與供應鏈需求 。摩根大通資產管理指出 ，納
入資料中心開發商及半導體投資後，AI 基礎建設至 2030 年的
累計投資需求可能達約 5兆美元 ，顯示AI投資循環仍具規模 ，
但實際經濟貢獻與企業獲利 ，仍取決於投資效率及商業化成果 。
• 外部融資需求增加 ，債券供給與投資回報成關鍵： 隨AI投資規
模擴大，大型科技企業逐步增加公司債與外部融資 ，在企業債
與公債供給同步增加下 ，長天期利率面臨上行壓力 。若未來AI
獲利成長未能跟上資本支出擴張速度 ，可能壓縮自由現金流與

資本報酬率，進一步擴大信用利差 ，並提高高估值科技股的評
價修正風險
• 市場觀察：AI基礎建設仍是未來數年推動全球科技投資與企業
數位轉型的成長主軸，相關需求具備中長期發展空間，而2026
年市場已逐漸轉向檢視投資回報率 、現金流創造能力及融資結
構是否具備可持續性 ，因此單純依賴資本支出擴張已不足以支
撐所有AI概念股評價 。建議配置可兼顧具獲利與現金流支撐的
AI供應鏈，以及高信用評等 、中短天期債券；對長天期債券則
須評估供給與利率風險 ，並審慎檢視高負債 AI企業的償債能力
及投資回報。
資料來源：Goldman Sachs，永豐金證券， 2026.10.12

AI熱潮、高債務與網路威脅，IMF示警三大金融穩定風險升高
資料來源：Bloomberg Finance L.P， Dealogic，Zero day Clock and IMF staff calculations.， 2026.10.13
AI相關風險持續升溫 主權債券殖利率攀升 網路資安威脅升高
大型雲端科技企業債券發行規模
（占投資等級公司債市場比重) 10年期公債殖利率平均變動幅度 零時差漏洞占比（%）
• AI估值高度集中，市場重定價風險升溫：IMF於10月《全球金融穩定報告》指出，AI相關投資已成為全球成長的重要支撐，
但資金與漲勢高度集中於少數企業及市場，一旦獲利成長不如預期或估值遭重新檢視，可能引發科技股修正並透過風險偏好
下降擴散至整體金融市場。
• 通膨與財政疑慮恐推升債券市場波動： IMF認為，若能源價格推升通膨壓力或市場對財政永續性產生疑慮 ，主權債殖利率可

能進一步上升，進而提高全球融資成本與資產重估風險；在高利率與高債務環境下，金融條件快速緊縮的情況將明顯提高。
• 網路安全風險成為潛在威脅： 隨金融體系數位化程度提高 ，IMF將大型網路攻擊列為重要風險來源之一 。若攻擊事件波及支
付系統、金融基礎設施或雲端服務平台，不僅可能影響市場運作效率，也可能透過信心衝擊與流動性壓力擴大金融市場波動。
• 投資建議：市場成長動能仍受AI支撐，但在金融穩定風險上升情境下，建議採取「AI受惠股＋高品質債券＋防禦型資產」的
槓鈴策略；核心配置可聚焦全球AI基礎建設供應鏈及投資等級債，藉由企業長期獲利成長與債券避險功能，同時因應估值修
正及金融條件收緊風險。

AI隱憂浮現但動能仍在
• CoreWeave信用風險亮紅燈：市場憂心雲端業者在算力租賃業務產生足夠回報前，恐難以負擔龐大債務，導致信用風險升
溫。上週，AI雲端業者CoreWeave的5年期信用違約交換（CDS）利差一度突破1,140個基點，升至通常反映嚴重信用壓力
的水準；旗下兩檔債券殖利率逼近15%，其中2032年到期的無擔保債券於上週三跌破面值的80%，相關賣壓並進一步蔓延
至股票市場。
• AI半導體產業的基本面仍具韌性：全球最大半導體設備商艾司摩爾（ASML）表示，客戶已開始洽談2029至2030年的訂單；
晶圓代工龍頭台積電財報亦優於市場預期。與此同時，台灣出口增速創3月以來新高，韓國9月半導體出口年增逾260%，顯
示半導體需求持續強勁，預期第三季相關企業獲利仍有望維持強勁。

美銀美林調查指出，全球經理人對景氣與股市仍維持偏多立場
資料來源：BofA Global Fund Manager Survey，2026.10
• 經理人樂觀情緒維持高檔： 美銀美林10月全球基金經理人調查顯示 ，市場風險偏好仍高 ，平均現金水位維持在 3.9%的低檔，
科技股配置則升至2020年8月以來最高，反映經理人對AI產業及企業獲利前景仍具信心 。然而，整體經濟成長預期已轉趨保
守，淨4%的受訪者預期未來 12個月經濟成長放緩 。極端風險方面，債券殖利率無序攀升仍居首位 ，占比31%；AI泡沫及通
膨再起分別為23%及20%。此外，39%的經理人認為，大型雲端業者資本支出是系統性信用事件的主要潛在來源 ，顯示市場
焦點已從AI需求成長延伸至龐大資本支出的獲利回收能力。

• 美國公債殖利率走高 ，債券的收益吸引力逐漸回升： 調查顯示，37%的受訪者表示，當美國10年期公債殖利率升至 6%時，
才會考慮由股票轉向債券；另有19%認為目前殖利率已具吸引力，顯示部分經理人已開始重新評估固定收益資產的配置價值 。
不過，債券整體仍處淨減碼，且經理人對科技股的配置持續升高 ，反映市場尚未全面轉向防禦。後續仍須關注美債殖利率變
化、AI資本支出的投資回報，以及企業獲利能否支撐偏高的市場評價。
資料來源：BofA Global Fund Manager Survey，2026.10

台積電財報佳卻未激勵股價，短線面臨總經干擾
• 台積電Q3營收、獲利及EPS同步改寫單季新高：營收年增50.9%、季增17.6%，稅後純益7,630.6億元，年增68.7％、季增8
％；每股稅後純益(EPS)29.43元，整體優於市場預期，但毛利率66.4％較第二季的67.7％下降。
• 財測展望：第四季財測營收預估 523~543億美元，季增上看15％、年增65％，毛利率預估65%~67%，反映高階晶片需求
依然強勁。全年展望方面，預期2026年美元營收年增約44%，較上季預估的「略高於40%」說法更加明確。此外，重申全
年資本支出600~640億美元不變。
• 市場反應：台積電法說會隔日股價小幅下跌，法說會未出現激勵行情，主要原因包含美國通膨升溫壓力、外資連續調節部位，

以及毛利率略為下降。短線籌碼面可能仍會調整，但台積電董事長魏哲家在法說會中強調，第二波AI需求正強勁興起，台積
電正透過多種途徑全力擴充產能，顯示高階晶片前景明確，中長期具投資價值，多家投資機構也紛紛上調目標價。
資料來源：台積電法說會，兆豐銀行整理，2026.10.15。
法說會重點整理 說明
AI需求持續升溫
魏哲家坦言，目前仍難以判斷晶圓供需缺口將擴大或縮小，因為
客戶需求持續增加，台積電也正積極提高產能。儘管消費性電子
市場仍受到零組件漲價及總體經濟不確定性影響，但先進製程需
求依然強勁，公司預估2026年全年美元營收將年增約44％。
擴廠進度
(1)台南科學園區新建3奈米廠預計2027年下半年量產，並決定在
台南再增建一座3奈米廠，規畫2028年投入量產。
(2)美國亞利桑那州第二座晶圓廠已完成廠房興建，預計2027年
第三季量產，時程較原先規畫略為提前；日本第二座晶圓廠則將
導入3奈米製程，預計2028年量產。
台積電證實洽談Terafab
合作
魏哲家強調，目前相關合作仍在討論階段，尚未有結論。不過，

無論未來採取何種合作模式，晶圓廠都必須由台積電負責營運。
電力供應疑慮
美國電力供應情況是公司評估AI長期需求的重要因素之一，因此
台積電進一步確認當地電力供應狀況，目前相關研究及評估結果
均良好，尚未發現明顯問題。

• 全球經濟正處於「韌性仍在、風險升高」的階段：國際貨幣基金組織(IMF)指出，AI投資熱潮持續推動美國、台灣等國家成
長，但中東地緣政治衝突、高通膨風險、政府債務問題與AI泡沫化疑慮，也同時為市場埋下不確定因素。
• 市場永遠充滿雜音，定期定額紀律投資更具優勢：本許多人認為定期定額的核心在於「平均成本」，但更重要的是幫助投資
人克服人性。市場上漲時持續投入，可以參與企業獲利成長帶來的成果，市場下跌時持續扣款，則能以較低價格累積更多單
位數。換句話說，定期定額讓投資人不需要每天判斷：明天會不會跌？現在是不是太貴？要不要先停扣觀望？長期而言，紀

律投資往往比短期擇時的成效更佳。
• AI時代更適合定期定額：儘管IMF認為人工智慧發展為全球經濟成長帶來貢獻，但同時也警告，若市場對AI未來獲利能力過
度樂觀，不排除出現股市估值修正。根據歷史經驗，每一次市場重大修正往往也孕育下一輪成長契機，不論AI發展是否出現
泡沫，善用定期定額策略可以在機會與風險之間達到平衡。
韌性仍在、風險升溫，紀律投資更穩健
任一月份定期定額進場投資試算
註：以MSCI全球指數2010/1/31~2025/12/31試算。假設定期定額每月月底投入、
原幣計算。指數資料試算僅供參考，非投資商品之實際績效。資料來源Bloomberg。
短進短出錯失行情的代價大
註：以美國標普500指數為試算範例，期間為1980年~2025年。資料來源Bloomberg。
投資人無法直接投資指數，資訊僅供歷史統計說明，非預測投資商品之績效。

4. 本資料所載內容如涉及商品資訊 ，商品相關資訊仍以經核可之條款及文宣 (DM)為主，本行網站已備有公開說明書可供查閱基金應負擔之費用及相關投資風險 。投
資人申購基金係持有基金受益憑證，而非本資料提及之投資資產或標的。
5. 本資料所載內容取自本公司相信可靠之資料來源，且為特定日期之判斷，本公司不保證其即時性、精確性及完整性，亦不提供或嘗試遊說客戶做為投資之判斷依據。

資料內容邇後若有變更，本公司將不做預告或主動更新。投資人於決策時應審慎衡量本身風險，如因信賴本資料內容而做出或改變投資決策，須自行承擔結果，本
公司及本公司所屬員工，均不負任何損害賠償及其他法律上之責任。
6. 本資料內容之著作權屬兆豐銀行所有，禁止就所載資料任何形式之抄襲、引用或轉載。
7. 本資料所載部分基金涉及非投資等級債，投資人投資以非投資等級債券為訴求之基金不宜占其投資組合過高之比重 。基金經金管會核准，惟不表示絕無風險。由於
非投資等級債券之信用評等未達投資等級或未經信用評等，且對利率變動的敏感度甚高，故本基金可能會因利率上升、市場流動性下降，或債券發行機構違約不支
付本金、利息或破產而蒙受虧損。部分基金不適合無法承擔相關風險之投資人。基金經理公司以往之經理績效不保證基金之最低投資收益；基金經理公司除盡善良
管理人之注意義務外不負責本基金之盈虧，亦不保證最低之收益，投資人申購前應詳閱基金公開說明書。
8. 基金年化配息率計算公式：每單位配息金額 ÷除息日前一日之淨值×一年配息次數×100%。「年化配息率」為估算值。當月配息率計算公式：每單位配息金額 ÷除

息日前一日之淨值×100%。
9. 基金配息率不代表基金報酬率，且過去配息率不代表未來配息率；基金淨值可能因市場因素而上下波動。投資人因不同時間進場，將有不同之投資績效，過去之績
效亦不代表未來績效之保證。本資料如以基金過去績效進行模擬投資組合之報酬率時，僅為歷史資料模擬投資組合之結果，不代表本投資組合之實際報酬率及未來
績效保證，不同時間進行模擬操作，其結果亦可能不同。
10. 部分基金配息前未先扣除應負擔之相關費用。基金的配息可能由基金的收益或本金中支付。任何涉及由本金支出的部份，可能導致原始投資金額減損。各基金由本
金支付配息之相關資料，投資人可至各投信基金公司及總代理人網站查詢。

2  兆豐國際商業銀行 http://www.megabank.com.tw/
上週重要市場資訊
 澳洲 8 月就業人數勁增 3.95 萬人大幅優於預期，但勞動參與率上升至 67.1%，
推升失業率至 4.6%，呈現勞動力擴增但市場邊際降溫跡象。澳洲央行總裁布拉
克日前指出，失業率回升至5%有助於抑制頑固通膨。考量物價長期高於目標中
點，市場預期澳洲央行本週二將重啟緊縮，升息 1 碼至 4.60%，創 2011年底以
來新高。
 瑞士央行（SNB）上週四宣布連續第五次將關鍵政策利率維持在0%不變，符合
市場預期。儘管受油價推升使 8 月通膨微升至 0.8%，但仍處於目標區間，央行
預期中期通膨壓力溫和，現行極度寬鬆立場依然適當。決議公布後瑞郎兌美元跌
至 1.2077。相較全球主要央行相繼緊縮，瑞士央行維持超低利率，策略轉為視情
況調節匯市以穩定瑞郎。
 標普全球公布歐元區 9 月綜合 PMI 初值升至 53.1，創三年新高，製造與服務業
同步擴張，新訂單增速達四年多最快，印證經濟無懼美伊衝突引發的能源成本飆

升，展現超預期韌性。受能源價格傳導加速影響，通膨壓力再度升溫，儘管尚未
浮現薪資二輪效應，但強勁景氣數據促使市場大幅提高歐洲央行提前於10月例
會再度升息的押注。
 美國 9 月綜合 PMI 初值勁升至 58.4，創 62 個月新高；製造業與服務業 PMI 亦
逆勢走強至 57與 58.7，凸顯企業擴張強勁，引發市場對通膨持續性的擔憂。市
場焦點轉向 9 月 30日公布的美國 8 月 PCE、個人消費支出與 Q2 GDP終值，同
日日本亦將發布短觀調查與央行意見摘要。若終端消費延續強勁，將牽動美日央
行政策利率預期。

3  兆豐國際商業銀行 http://www.megabank.com.tw/ 
 美元兌新台幣匯率走勢  
Sep/29/2026

＜上週走勢＞ 
上週台幣匯價高點在 31.655，低點在 31.866，以 31.790作收，較前一周收盤價
升值 2.8分。 
上週台幣先升後貶，周初，台股創新高，外資連續五日買超台股並匯入，推升
台幣最高來到 31.655，不過逢低吸引進口商、政府基金等買盤進場，台幣主要在
31.700 附近震盪；臨近周末，美國 9 月綜合 PMI 由 8 月的 56 走升至 58.4，創下逾
5 年來的新高，加上美伊戰事持續，皆推升市場對後續升息的預期，美元指數升至
101 上方，台幣跳空開低，回貶至 31.800 上方，最低來到 31.866，不過隨後出口商
賣壓出籠，台幣跌勢放緩，最終在央行調節之下收在 31.790。 
＜未來分析＞ 
     預估本週台幣區間震盪。就國際市場而言，美國經濟表現仍具韌性，9月綜合
PMI 由 8 月的 56 上升至 58.4，企業活動加速至逾 5 年來最強，數據公布後美國 10
年債券殖利率升破 5%，Fed Watch 顯示市場對於聯準會 10 月升息預期升高，有助
於美元指數維持偏強格局，預計將給台幣帶來壓力；就台灣市場來看，台股在創下
新高後回落，而外資也結束連續五日買超的走勢，轉為賣超，短線上資金匯入不利
台幣表現，然而，台灣8 月外銷訂單達 1,029.6億美元、年增 71.4%，創歷史新高，
AI 需求帶動出口持續強勁，出口商後續拋匯需求預計將提供台幣支撐。綜合以上，
預估本週台幣區間震盪。 
＜預估本週＞ 美元兌台幣：31.600-32.000

美元兌人民幣匯率走勢  
Sep/29/2026 
＜上週走勢＞ 
上週人民幣震盪走貶。人民幣中間價在上周連續十天走升，並升破6.7500整數
關口，最高來到 6.7468，帶動人民幣偏升，不過由於美伊戰事持續，市場對於聯準
會升息預期提升，美元指數震盪走強，加上週間公布美國9月綜合 PMI由 8 月的 56
走升至 58.4，創下逾 5 年來的新高，美元指數突破 101 整數關卡，令境外人民幣承
壓，USD/CNH回貶至 6.7200上方，終場境外人民幣收在6.7227，週線貶值283點，
境內人民幣則收在 6.7125，週線貶值 141 點。

＜未來分析＞ 
     預估本週人民幣高檔震盪。就國際市場來看，美中兩國完成新一輪談判，美方
表示雙方同意將原訂 11 月 10 日到期的貿易休戰延長至明年 1 月 10 日，並持續就
關稅、貿易及供應鏈等議題進行協商，降低短期貿易摩擦升溫的風險，對人民幣形
成一定支撐。不過，美國近期經濟數據仍具韌性，加以聯準會偏鷹立場推升美元，
預計將限制人民幣升值空間；就中國國內市場來看，近期人行持續偏強設定中間價，
帶動人民幣維持偏強走勢，惟美中利差再度擴大，預計將限制人民幣升值的空間；
最後就技術面來看，USD/CNH日線圖中，匯價反彈至 20日均線之上，顯示原先下
行動能暫歇，上方壓力先看向季線 6.7350附近，綜合以上，預估本週人民幣高檔震
盪。

＜預估本週＞ 美元兌人民幣：6.6900-6.7400

5  兆豐國際商業銀行 http://www.megabank.com.tw/ 
美元兌日圓匯率走勢  
         Sep/29/2026

＜上週走勢＞ 
上週 USD/JPY先上後下。週初，美國多位聯準會官員鷹派談話，帶動美元指數
持續上行，推升 USD/JPY延續前一週的走勢來到 157上方；週間，受美國 PMI 強
於預期與美債殖利率全面上行帶動，市場重新提高對聯準會後續升息路徑的押注，
帶動 USD/JPY大漲突破 159的關卡；臨近週末，日本首相高市早苗表態日圓被低估
是個問題，使市場提高對政策訊號與潛在干預的敏感度，帶動USD/JPY回吐大部分
漲幅，終場收在 157.26，週線上漲 56點。 
＜未來分析＞ 
  預估本週 USD/JPY區間震盪，主要原因如下：一、日本財務大臣片山皋月上週
五罕見透露，美國總統川普在美日元首峰會上對日圓過度疲弱表達關切，首相高市
早苗亦回應日圓遭低估確屬問題。儘管近期美元指數維持在 101 上方，但在日本政
府可能干預的陰影下，預計將限縮日圓貶值的空間。二、美國9 月綜合 PMI產出指
數創逾五年新高，疊加美伊戰事推升油價突破100美元與柴油大漲，通膨壓力劇增。
美聯儲理事巴爾表態可能需再升息兩次，芝加哥聯儲古爾斯比也示警能源衝擊具持
續性，推升市場押注 10 月升息機率升至六成，預計將支撐 USD/JPY 走勢。三、就
技術面而言，日線圖中，USD/JPY連日上漲一度站回年線但隨即失守，目前依舊承
壓於季線及年線下方，不過各天期均線趨平，且下方尚有月線支撐，短線尚無明確
的方向。綜合以上，預估 USD/JPY本週呈現區間震盪的可能性較高。

美元兌歐元匯率走勢  
             Sep/29/2026

＜上週走勢＞ 
  上週 EUR/USD 下跌。週初，歐元延續前一週偏弱的走勢，加上聯準會官員鷹
派發言，導致歐元跌破 1.1450的重要關卡；週間，儘管油價自高檔回落，但市場解
讀為能源成本仍處高位、難以迅速回落，推升對美國實質利率與利差優勢的預期。
加上歐洲在能源依賴與成長動能較弱下，相對於美國更難承受長期高利率環境，歐
元最低來到 1.1358；臨近週末，歐元跌深反彈，終場收在1.1391，週線下跌 91點。 
＜未來分析＞ 
  預估本週 EUR/USD 震盪偏弱，主要原因如下：一、歐洲央行首席經濟學家連
恩示警，第二波能源價格飆升將推升通膨，預計至2027年中期始回落至目標水準。
德國央行月報指出，受中東衝突、低天然氣庫存及明年醫療改革影響，通膨將居高
不下；夏季雖因出口疲弱與乾旱致增長趨緩，但在製造業回暖與財政支出挹注下，
估年底經濟有望重啟復甦，預計將提供歐元一定的支撐。二、美聯儲官員接連釋放
強烈鷹派信號，聖路易聯儲總裁穆薩萊姆直言當前 3.75%-4.00%利率仍偏寬鬆，呼
籲盡早收緊；芝加哥聯儲總裁古爾斯比亦警告，AI投資繁榮引發需求過熱，疊加持
續性供應衝擊，可能迫使美聯儲加速且更積極地升息。目前Fedwatch顯示預期明年
4 月前可能再升息三次，將不利歐元表現。三、技術面而言，在日線圖中，EUR/USD
在各天期均線下方，且接連失守1.15及 1.14整數關卡，空方動能較佔優勢。綜合以
上，預估 EUR/USD本週呈現震盪偏弱的可能性較高。

7  兆豐國際商業銀行 http://www.megabank.com.tw/ 
                           新台幣利率走勢分析

115.09.21-115.09.24 
銀行隔夜拆款利率 0.804% ~ 0.830%  
30天期短票利率   1.65% ~ 1.69%  
公債－10 年期-A15109     1.95%  
－ 5 年期- A15108 1.84%

一、 台幣貨幣市場部分，本周央行持續每日減發定存單，令市場資金充裕，帶動短
率靠向區間低位。 
週二央行標售 2 年期定存單 250 億元，加權平均得標利率為 1.638%，略低於
市場預期，且較上次標售結果下跌 2.5個基本點。 
週四隔夜拆款利率區間在 0.804%~0.830%， 30 天期短票主要成交在
1.65%~1.69%，利率較上週下跌 1 個基本點。 
二、 本週市場交投冷清，10 年期指標券 A15109 及 5 年期指標券 A15108 均未成
交，週四等殖系統參考報價中價分別為 1.95%、1.84%。 
台股排擠效應、國際債市走勢偏空，加上油價續處高檔，通膨潛在威脅仍存，
12 月升息可能性未能完全排除，市場氣氛續偏觀望，預期台債殖利率將維持
高檔區間盤整 ， 預估5年券區間為 1.84%~1.90%，10年券區間為 1.95%~2.0%。

產業速報  
                                        2026 年 10 月 14 日

1 群益投顧 
京元電 (2449 TT) 宣 布 與 NVIDIA  International, Inc. 簽訂預付款協議，支持美國全資
子公司 AeternZ 於 當 地 建 置 產 能 ， 但 現 階 段 仍 未 揭 露 合 作 金 額。 NVIDIA 於 07/2026
與 Amkor (AMKR US) 也 簽 約 相 似 的 預 付 款 合 約。顯 示 AI 晶 片 由 傳 統 單 一 系 統 單 晶 片
(SoC) 朝 Chiplet 及 異 質 整 合 發 展 ，封 測 複 雜 度 提 升 ，IC 設 計 廠 和 封 測 廠 合 作 深 化，
提 高 後 段 封 測 廠 訂 單 能 見 度 。 不 過 和NVIDIA 合作的此協議短期效益有限，京元電評
價合理 。

研 究 員 ： 陳 俐 妍   
ly.c@capital.com.tw  
京 元 電 宣 布 與 NVIDIA 簽 訂 預 付 款 協 議 ， 支 持 美 國 全 資 子 公 司 AeternZ 於
當 地 建 置 產 能，但 現 階 段 仍 未 揭 露 合 作 金 額：  
10/13/2026 京 元 電 重 訊 公 告 ， 董 事 會 決 議 通 過 與NVIDIA(NVDA US) 簽 訂 客
戶 預 付 款 協 議，將 由 NVIDIA 預 先 支 付 款 項，支 持 京 元 電 及 美 國 全 資 子 公 司
AeternZ Semiconductor Testing Corporation 在 美 國 建 置 測 試 產 能 ， 未 來 再 以
提 供 測 試 服 務 抵 減 預 付 款。京 元 電 同 步 宣 布，因 合 約 架 構 調 整，取 消 原 先 對
美 國 子 公 司 提 供 的 3 億 美 元 背 書 保 證 額 度 。 
京 元 電 此 次簽約 對 象 為 NVIDIA International, Inc. ， NVIDIA 預 付 款 項 ， 京
元 電 及 其 美 國 全 資 子 公 司 將 在 美 國 建 置 測 試 產 能 ， 為 NVIDIA 保 留 產 能 並
提 供 測 試 服 務，預 付 款 則 以 未 來 服 務 款 項 抵 減。此 合 作 並 非 股 權 投 資 或 合 資
模 式，而 是 在 既 有 客 戶 關 係 下，透 過 客 戶 提 前 提 供 資 金，支 持 供 應 商 擴 充 產
能 並 確 保 未 來 測 試 資 源 。 
京 元 電 董 事 會 已 於 07/ 2026 決 議 通 過 美 國 投 資 設 廠 案，規 劃 在 美 國 設 立 孫 公
司 並 興 建 新 廠 ， 投 資 金 額 以 14 億美元 (約 新 台 幣 450 億元 )為 上 限 。 公 司 當
時 表 示，此 投 資 主 要 因 應 未 來 營 運 需 求 及 全 球 供 應 鏈 布 局，後 續 將 依 設 廠 進
度 與 資 金 需 求 逐 步 投 入 ， 資 金 來 源 以 自 有 資 金 或 借 款 等 方 式 支 應 。 
此 次 協 議 有 助 於 提 高 新 增 產 能 的 需 求 能 見 度，並 降 低 部 分 初 期 資 金 壓 力。不
過，由 於 預 付 款 金 額、實 際 保 留 產 能 規 模 及 相 關 訂 單 金 額 尚 未 公 開，現 階 段
仍 無 法 量 化 對 營 收 與 獲 利 的 貢 獻；短 期 營 運 表 現 仍 取 決 於 既 有 產 能 擴 充、新
晶 片 測 試 需 求 及 美 國 廠 建 置 進 度 。 
NVIDIA 在 07/2026 已和 Amkor(AMKR US) 簽 訂 預 付 款 協 議 ， 約 15 億美
元 ：  
值 得 注 意 的 是 ，NVIDIA 於 07/ 2026 與 Amkor 簽訂約 15 億 美 元 的 多 年 期 先
進 封 裝 與 測 試 合 作 協 議，包 括 正 在 亞 利 桑 那 州 建 設 的 生 產 設 施。Amkor 目前
已為 NVIDIA 資 料 中 心 處 理 器 、 網 通 晶 片 組 及 加 速 運 算 系 統 提 供 先 進 封 裝
服務 。  
Amkor 於 2Q 26 法 說 會 表 示 ， 相 關 預 付 款 預 計 於 2027 年 收 取 ， 並 隨 後 續 提
供 服 務 逐 步 抵 減 ， 依 實 際 生 產 規 模 及 服 務 提 供 速 度 ， 預 估 合 作 期 間 約5~10
年 。 此 案 例 顯 示 ，NVIDIA 透 過 預 付 款 支 持 封 測 廠 擴 充 美 國 產 能 ， 並 非 短 期
採 購 安 排 ， 而 可 能 是 配 合 長 期 產 能 建 置 及 技 術 發 展 的 合 作 模 式 。 
京 元 電 此 次 亦 採 取和 Amkor 相 似 的 方 式 ，客 戶 預 付 款 、 建 置 美 國 產 能 並 以
未 來 測 試 服 務 款 項 抵 減。 Amkor 案 例 可 作 為 評 估 合 作 期 間 的 參 考。  
2 家 不 同 之 處，京 元 電 主 要 受 惠 於 高 階 測 試 需 求；Amkor 則 涉 及 先 進 封 裝 與

2 群益投顧 
測 試。兩 者 都 能 提 高 產 能 需 求 能 見 度，但 實 際 營 收 與 獲 利 貢 獻 仍 取 決 於 產 能
開 出 、 訂 單 規 模 及 服 務 條 件 。 
外 媒 報 導 京 元 電 美 國 子 公 司預 計 落 腳 德 州 沃 斯 堡 市(Fort Worth )：  
在 美 國 設 廠 地 點 方 面， 外 媒 於10/ 2026 報 導， 京 元 電 全 資 美 國 子 公 司AeternZ
正 評 估 於 德 州 沃 斯 堡(Fort Worth )設 立 半 導 體 測 試 廠，初 步 規 劃 投 資 約 13 億
美 元 ， 其 中 約 10 億 美 元 用 於 不 動 產 、3 億 美 元 用 於 設 備 ， 選 址 涉 及 West 
Everman Parkway 一 處 約 23 英 畝 的 土 地 。 沃 斯 堡 市 議 會 預 計 於 10 /27/2026
審 議 相 關 租 稅 優 惠，但 目 前 尚 不 能 視 為 京 元 電 已 正 式 決 定 設 廠 地 點，仍 須 觀
察 後 續 選 址 及 投 資 進 度。若 該 地 點 獲 選，德州相對 台 積 電 所 在 的 亞 利 桑 那 州
鳳 凰 城 聚 落 ，地 緣 上 相 對 鄰 近 AI 伺 服 器 供 應 鏈 ，代 表 AI 晶 片 完 成 測 試 後，
可 就 近 送 往 當 地 代 工 廠 ， 進 行 整 機 組 裝 與 系 統 測 試 。不 過 ， 廠 房 建 置 、 設 備
進 駐 及 客 戶 認 證 仍 需 時 間 ， 實 際 貢 獻 時 點 取 決 於 建 廠 進 度 與 測 試 需 求 。 
AI 晶 片 由 傳 統 單 一 系 統 單 晶 片(SoC) 朝 Chiplet 及 異 質 整 合 發 展， 封 測 複 雜
度 提 升 ， IC 設 計 廠 和 封 測 廠 合 作 深 化：  
隨著 AI 晶 片 由 傳 統 單 一 系 統 單 晶 片(SoC ) 朝 Chiplet 及 異 質 整 合 發 展 ， 半 導
體 產 業 的 競 爭 已 不 再 侷 限 於 晶 片 設 計 與 先 進 製 程，也 延 伸 至 先 進 封 裝、晶 粒
間 高 速 互 連、散 熱 及 測 試 驗 證 等 環 節。晶 片 架 構 複 雜 度 提 高，使 封 裝 與 測 試
成 為 影 響 產 品 效 能 、 成 本 及 量 產 能 力 的 重 要 因 素 。 
對 京 元 電 而 言，若 能 持 續 參 與 NVIDIA 新 晶 片 的 測 試 開 發 與 驗 證，並 承 接 後
續 量 產 測 試，有 助 於 深 化 客 戶 關 係 及 鞏 固 高 階 測 試 業 務 的 競 爭 優 勢；但 此 次
預付款協議本身並未改變雙方的股權關係，合作價值仍須透過後續訂單與
產 能 利 用 率 驗 證 。 
相 較 之 下 ， 力 成(6239 TT) 與 Broadcom (AVGO US) 規 劃 在 新 加 坡 成 立 先 進 封
裝 合 資 公 司 ， 發 展 扇 出 型 面 板 級 封 裝 （FOPLP ）及 重 布 線 層 （RDL ） 等 相 關
技術，屬於 IC 設 計 公 司 直 接 參 與 封 裝 製 造 平 台 投 資 的 模 式 。 此 合 作 不 僅 涉
及 產 能 配 置，也 涵 蓋 新 技 術 開 發 與 量 產 導 入，反 映 當 先 進 封 裝 成 為 影 響 晶 片
效 能 、 互 連 密 度 及 量 產 成 本 的 關 鍵 環 節 ，IC 設 計 公 司 可 能 更 積 極 參 與 封 裝
技 術 與 產 能 布 局。對 力 成 而 言，若 能 順 利 完 成 製 程 驗 證、良 率 提 升 及 量 產 導
入，有 機 會 拓 展 新 的 技 術 平 台 與 業 務 成 長 機 會；惟 仍 須 觀 察 客 戶 訂 單、量 產
進 度 、 資 本 支 出 及 投 資 報 酬 率 。 
整 體 而 言 ，NVIDIA 與 京 元 電 、Amkor 的 合 作 ， 主 要 是 透 過 客 戶 預 付 款 支 持
產 能 建 置 ， 強 化 供 應 商 的 需 求 與 資 金 能 見 度 ；Broadcom 與 力 成 的 合 作 ， 則
是 透 過 共 同 投 資 發 展 新 的 先 進 封 裝 技 術 平 台 。 兩 者 反 映AI 半 導 體 產 業 的 合
作 模 式 正 逐 步 深 化，但 資 金 安 排、技 術 參 與 程 度 及 投 資 風 險 並 不 相 同。投 資
上，京 元 電 應 關 注 預 付 款 規 模、美 國 新 廠 投 產 及 高 階 測 試 訂 單 成 長；力 成 則
應 觀 察 先 進 封 裝 技 術 商 業 化、量 產 良 率 與 合 資 事 業 的 投 資 報 酬。京 元 電 此 次
協 議 對 中 長 期 業 務 發 展 具 正 面 意 義，但 在 實 際 財 務 貢 獻 尚 未 明 朗 前，宜 視 為
客 戶 關 係 深 化 及 產 能 布 局 的 正 面 訊 號，而 非 直 接 上 修 獲 利 預 估 的 依 據。由於
目 前 京 元 電 評 價 合 理 ，投 資 建 議 Neutral 。

### Page 4

產業速報  
                                        2026 年 10 月 14 日

4 群益投顧 
  【 投 資 評 等 說 明 】 
  評等  定義  
  強力買進 (Strong Buy)  首 次 評 等 潛 在 上 漲 空 間≥ 35%  
  買進 (Buy)  15%≤ 首 次 評 等 潛 在 上 漲 空 間<35%  
  區間操作 (Trading Buy)  5%≤ 首 次 評 等 潛 在 上 漲 空 間<15%

中立 (Neutral)  
無 法 由 基 本 面 給 予 投 資 評 等 
預 期 近 期 股 價 將 處 於 盤 整 
建 議 降 低 持 股

【 投 資 評 等 調 整 機 制 】 
  投 資 評 等 係「 首 次 」給 予 特 定 個 股 投 資 評 等 時，其 前 一 日 收 盤 價 相 較3 個月
目 標 價 之 潛 在 上 漲 空 間 計 算 而 得 。 個 股 投 資 評 等 分 為四 個 等 級 ， 定 義 如 上 。
爾 後 的 投 資 評 等係 依 循 「 首 次 評 等 」 ， 直 到 停 止 推 薦 。  
「 強 力 買 進 」、「 買 進 」及 「 區 間 操 作 」均有 upside 目 標 價 。差 別 僅 在 於 ，
首 次 評 等 時 潛 在 報 酬 率 不 同 。 「 中 立 」 則 無 目 標 價 。 
一 旦 我 們 給 予 特 定 個 股「 強 力 買 進 」、「 買 進 」或「 區 間 操 作 」之 投 資 評 等，
就 是 責 任 的 開 始，爾 後 將 透 過 各 式 研 究 報 告 作 定 期 性、持 續 性 追 蹤 基 本 面 及
股 價 變 化 ， 直 到 停 止 推 薦 。 
停 止 推 薦 情 境 ： 
1.  達 目 標 價 。 
2.  雖 未 達 原 訂 目 標 價 ， 但 檢 視 基 本 面 、 訊 息 面 、 籌 碼 面 等 多 方 訊 息 ， 研
判 股 價 上 漲 空 間 已 然 有 限 ， 將 適 時 出 具 降 評 報 告 。 
3.  推 薦 後 股 價 不 漲 反 跌 ， 亦 將 出 具 降 評 報 告 。

【免責聲明】  
  本 研 究 報 告 僅 提 供 予 特 定 人 之 客 戶 作 為 參 考 資 料「 非 經 同 意 不 得 轉 載 」 。我
們 並 不 確 保 此 資 訊 的 完 整 性 與 正 確 性，投 資 人 應 了 解，報 告 中 有 關 未 來 預 測
之 陳 述 可 能 不 會 實 現，因 而 不 應 被 依 賴。而 且 此 報 告 並 非 根 據 特 定 投 資 目 的
或 依 預 定 對 象 之 財 務 狀 況 所 撰 寫 出 來 的，因 此，此 研 究 報 告 的 目 的，既 非 對
投 資 人 於 買 賣 證 券、選 擇 權、期 貨 或 其 他 證 券 相 關 之 衍 生 性 商 品 提 供 詢 價 服
務，亦 非 作 為 進 行 交 易 的 要 約。投 資 人 應 注 意 到 相 關 證 券 之 價 值 及 收 益，可
能會有無預警地上升或下降，產生投資回報金額可能比原始投資來得少的
情形。

September Sales Flat MoM/

In this report, we focus on Hiwin's monthly sales, which we
believe could be a catalyst for its share price.
Details:
• September sales were NT$2,720mn (flat MoM/+30% YoY).
• 3Q26 revenue came in at NT$8,110mn (+8% QoQ/+35% YoY), largely in line
with our estimate at NT$7,996mn (+6% QoQ/+33% YoY) and the  consensus
of NT$7,964mn (+6% QoQ/+33% YoY).
Our view:
• On top of the sequential growth on the top line, we believe that Hiwin

should also further expand its gross margin on a QoQ basis in 3Q26, owing
to the higher utilization and the tailwinds of price hikes.
• We think the momentum should remain favorable in the near term.
• We stay constructive on the stock, with current valuation at 29x 2027e P/E
vs. a peak cycle level of 35-40x.

Valuation Methodology and Risks
Hiwin Technologies Corp. (2049.TW)
Base case, P/E. We think this valuation methodology better captures Hiwin's earnings growth
momentum, as its valuation tends to move with the business cycle. We apply a target multiple
of 38x to our 2027 EPS estimate. We view this multiple as justified by the 47% operating profit
CAGR we project for 2025-28.
Risks to Upside
n Stronger industrial automation demand pickup.

n Market share gains.
n Earlier-than-expected revenue contribution from humanoids.
Risks to Downside
n Weaker industrial automation demand.
n More pricing pressure.
n Market share loss.
n More broad-based impact on the global economy from geopolitical risks.

Stanley Research, in its entirety, and not infer the contents from the rating alone.  In any case, ratings (or research) should not be used or relied upon as investment advice.  An investor's decision

Analyst Stock Ratings
Overweight (O). The stock's total return is expected to exceed the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next
12-18 months.
Equal-weight (E). The stock's total return is expected to be in line with the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over

the next 12-18 months.
Not-Rated (NR). Currently the analyst does not have adequate conviction about the stock's total return relative to the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage
universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next 12-18 months.
Underweight (U). The stock's total return is expected to be below the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next

12-18 months.
Unless otherwise specified, the time frame for price targets included in Morgan Stanley Research is 12 to 18 months.
Analyst Industry Views
Attractive (A): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be attractive vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated
below.
In-Line (I): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be in line with the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.

Cautious (C): The analyst views the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months with caution vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.
Benchmarks for each region are as follows: North America - S&P 500; Latin America - relevant MSCI country index or MSCI Latin America Index; Europe - MSCI Europe; Japan - TOPIX; Asia -
relevant MSCI country index or MSCI sub-regional index or MSCI AC Asia Pacific ex Japan Index.

3-month preview: The company’s Sep revenues were up 28% YoY/10% MoM to 
NT$224m, though 27% below our previous estimates, we attribute this to the 
longer-than-expected time to see high-speed CPO FAU ramp up. Looking ahead, we 
expect to see sequential MoM increase at 15%/20% MoM in Oct/Nov 2026 to 
NT$258m/ NT$309m, supported by capacity expansion, efficiency improvement, 
and rack-level AI server ramp up.

### Page 2

Earnings revision: Factoring in FOCI’s 3Q26 revenues, we revise down 2026E earnings 
by 6% on lower-than-expected CPO FAU revenues in 3Q, and revise up 2027E-30E 
earnings by 1%/1%/1%/1% mainly on higher revenues of high-speed CPO FAU, 
supported by FOCI’s focus on CPO-scale up and rising CPO switch demand.

Valuation: We continue to derive our 12m target price via a discounted P/E and derive 
the target P/E multiple via peers’ trading P/E vs. forward year EPS YoY (methodology 
unchanged). With this, our target multiple declines slightly to 16.4x (vs. 15.4x previously) 
based on FOCI’s largely unchanged long-term earnings growth (2030E-31E EPS YoY at 
15% on avg.), reflecting improving sentiment on rack-level AI server ramp up and 
high-speed transition. We continue to apply the target P/E to 2030E EPS (unchanged), 
and discount it back to 2027E via a 12.6% COE (unchanged). Our new TP is NT$900 (vs. 
NT$833 previously). Our new TP implies 51x 2027E P/E. Our new TP implies 10x 2027E 
P/S, within its historical trading range of 5x to 30x. Maintain Buy.

Interest in Connection with Investment Research.
Japan: Goldman Sachs Japan Co., Ltd. is a Financial Instrument Dealer registered with the Kanto Financial Bureau under registration number Kinsho 69,

Ratings, coverage universe and related definitions
Buy (B), Neutral (N), Sell (S) Analysts recommend stocks as Buys or Sells for inclusion on various regional Investment Lists. Being assigned a Buy or Sell
on an Investment List is determined by a stock’s total return potential relative to its coverage universe. Any stock not assigned as a Buy or a Sell on an
Investment List with an active rating (i.e., a  stock that is not Rating Suspended, Not Rated, Early-Stage Biotech, Coverage Suspended or Not Covered), is

deemed Neutral. Each region manages Regional Conviction Lists, which are selected from Buy rated stocks on the respective region’s Investment Lists
and represent investment recommendations focused on the size of the total return potential and/or the likelihood of the realization of the return across
their respective areas of coverage.  The addition or removal of stocks from such Conviction Lists are managed by the Investment Review Committee or
other designated committee in each respective region and do not represent a change in the analysts’ investment rating for such stocks.

Total return potential represents the upside or downside differential between the current share price and the price target, including all paid or
anticipated dividends, expected during the time horizon associated with the price target. Price targets are required for all covered stocks. The total
return potential, price target and associated time horizon are stated in each report adding or reiterating an Investment List membership.
Coverage Universe: A list of all stocks in each coverage universe is available by primary analyst, stock and coverage universe at

https://www.gs.com/research/hedge.html.
Not Rated (NR). The investment rating, target price and earnings estimates (where relevant) are removed pursuant to Goldman Sachs policy when
Goldman Sachs is acting in an advisory capacity in a merger or in a strategic transaction involving this company, when there are legal, regulatory or
policy constraints due to Goldman Sachs’ involvement in a transaction, and in certain other circumstances. Early-Stage Biotech (ES). An investment

rating and a target price are not assigned pursuant to Goldman Sachs policy when this company has neither a drug, treatment or medical device that
has passed a Phase II clinical trial nor a license to distribute a post-Phase II drug, treatment or medical device.  Rating Suspended (RS). Goldman Sachs
Research has suspended the investment rating and price target for this stock, because there is not a sufficient fundamental basis for determining an
investment rating or target price. The previous investment rating and target price, if any, are no longer in effect for this stock and should not be relied

upon.  Coverage Suspended (CS). Goldman Sachs has suspended coverage of this company.  Not Covered (NC). Goldman Sachs does not cover this
company.

元富證券投資顧問股份有限公司 台北市敦化南路二段 97 號 19 樓 (02)2325-3299  (1 15)金管投顧新字第 016 號

百和(9938) 
建議：維持買進 
結論：最壞已過且股價低於淨值，靜待基本面起飛

一、3Q26 自結 EPS 0.62 元，低於預期；主要受到網
布毛利率下滑且業外利息費用所致 
百和 3Q26 營收 44.09 億元(+15.9% QoQ / +5.8% YOY)，
就各產品比重來看，黏扣帶 12.3%、織鞋帶 44.1%、鬆
緊帶 13%、射出勾 6.6%、針織經編提花網布 20.1%以
及其他 3.9%。3Q26 就各產品線來看，所有產品線均成
長，僅有經編網布衰退，其中鬆緊帶+16%YoY、黏扣帶
+9.7%YoY、織鞋帶+9% YOY、射出勾+6.7%YoY 以及
針織經編提花網布-8.4% YOY。獲利方面，3Q26 毛利
率 29.5%，與 2Q26 約略持平，也較3Q25 下滑 6.3%，
主要反映關稅影響、產品組合以及受到美伊戰爭影響原
物料成本上揚所致；就 3Q26 各產品線毛利率來看，粘
扣帶 27.6%、 鬆緊帶37.9%、 射出勾44%、 織鞋帶30.4%、
經編網布 17.2％以及其他 37.5%。其中經編網布毛利率
較 2Q26 回升 2.7%，但仍低於 2025 年水準，主要反映
產品組合差異以及稼動率的影響；3Q26 合併營業利益
達 4.35 億元，營業利益率 9.9%，業外淨損 1.41 億元，
主要反映百和-KY 利息費用，稅前淨利 2.94 億元，稅後
歸屬母公司淨利 1.84 億元，EPS 0.62 元。  
二 、 考量原物料成本提升且通膨持續影響全球消費景氣 ，
下修百和 2026 年營收為低個位數增長且因毛利率表現
低於預期，下修 2026 年 EPS 為 2.48 元 
9M26 前十大客戶佔比 65.5%，德國品牌仍為第一大客
戶 ， 佔比25.1%，YOY-5%、 美系品牌15.7%，YOY+2%，
其中鞋子持平、服飾類+11%、瑞士品牌為第三大客戶，
佔營收比重 6.1%，YOY-11%，New Balance 占比 4.4%，
YOY-11%。HOKA 已為前十大客戶 ， 佔營收比重2.3%，
YOY+58%，為前九月成長性最佳客戶。就產品應用別
來看 ，前九月Footwear 占比 60.4%、Apparel 25.3%、
3C 2.8%； 以前九個月YOY 來看 ，Footwear-1% YOY、
Apparel +12% YOY、3C 產品+16% YOY 以及醫療-3% 
YOY，但單就第三季表現來看，Footwear 持平、
Apparel+27% YOY、3C+20% YOY 以及醫療+31% 
YOY，顯示其他應用端需求起來，未來百和將持續著重
其他類別發展，不單僅限於鞋子跟衣服，醫療跟 3C 已
漸有成效。過去 3C 是客人指定材料，百和僅有加工；
但現在幾乎 80%都是百和的材料進行加工，做成客戶想
要的東西，附加價值較高。3C 及醫療目前營收占比約
6%，未來可望緩步增加。 
考量美伊戰爭引發原物料成本提升且戰爭仍未平息影響
全球消費景氣，輔以客戶下單仍顯保守；我們預估百和
2026 年營收為 161.25 億元 (+2.2% YoY)，其中傳產中
個位數成長、網布中~高個位數衰退；毛利率方面，考量
原物料價格上揚因素未解、關稅影響持續且經編網布毛
利率低於預期 ， 保守預估2026 年集團毛利率為 30.7%，
較 2025 年下滑 4.6%，下修本業獲利為 17.68 億元，營
業利益率 11%，業外利息費用與 2025 年大致相當，預
估稅後歸屬母公司淨利潤 7.41 億元 ， 下修EPS 為 2.48
元。 
資本支出方面，2025 年實際資本支出 19.01 億元，其中
廠房建物 26％、設備購置 72%以及其他 2%。9M26 資
本支出 22.69 億元，其中台灣百和3.31 億元、百和興業
19.38 億元；2026 年預期資本支出約 28.02 億元，主要
以百和興業為主，約占 3/4；台灣百和約 1/4，主要用在
機器設備。 
三、下修經編緹花網布 2026 年至中~高個位數衰退，長
期仍可望有轉單效益 
2025 年隨著品牌採用意願提升，網布營收貢獻達 34.08
億元，YOY+38%，毛利率 34.09%；2026 年原預估在
新產能以及核心客戶、新興客戶放量且打入成衣供應鏈
帶動，成長性不亞於 2025 年，但受到全球大環境不確
定性高且受到產品組合以及主要客戶新一代跑鞋主料轉
用梭織且非由百和-KY 供應影響，前九個月網布營收衰
4.9%，我們下修 2026 年經編緹花網布營收為 31.46 億
元，-7.68% YOY， 毛利率受到稼動率偏低且產品組合影
響，前三季僅 19.9%。現在網布往圓編、橫編等更多織
法開發，未來可望持續擴大應用面，未來隨著應用面擴
大且稼動率提升，毛利率有機會往 2025 年水準邁進。 
網布客戶占比來看 ，2025 年德系品牌仍是第一大客戶，

四、維持買進理由 
2026 年受到美伊戰爭引發原物料成本提升且戰爭仍未
平息影響全球消費景氣，輔以客戶下單仍顯保守；我們
預估百和 2026 年營收為 161.25 億元 (+2.2% YoY)，其
中傳產中個位數成長 、 網布中~高個位數衰退 ， 下修EPS
為 2.48 元。展望 2027 年，隨著品牌對應庫存調整到較
健康的水位，下單可望回到正常模式。大環境穩定後，
原物料成本可以趨於正常、關稅議題減緩，2027 年可望
優於 2026 年，預估 2027 年 EPS 3.23 元。股價方面，
考量最壞已過 、2027 年可望恢復成長且目前股價低於淨
值，故維持買進評等，目標價 48 元(15X 2027PER)。

## Document 202: 9月金融数据点评：政策加力有望支撑四季度社融企稳

Source PDF: all report pdf/9月金融数据点评：政策加力有望支撑四季度社融企稳.pdf
Report source: Unknown
Date: 未知日期
Pages: 4
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

证券研究报告 
宏观经济点评 2026 年 10 月 15 日

请务必阅读正文之后的信息披露和法律声明

9 月金融数据点评：政策加力有望支撑四季度社融企稳

[Table_Author] 
 沈夏宜 分析师  魏争 分析师

Email:shenxiayi@lczq.com Email:weizheng@lczq.com  
证书:S1320523020004 证书:S1320524100001

### Page 2

宏观经济点评  
请务必阅读正文之后的信息披露和法律声明 2 
外币贷款同比多增。9 月新增外币贷款 349 亿元，同比多增 478 亿元，可
能与当月进口增速偏强，企业进口付汇需求增加有关。委托贷款同比多
增。9 月新增委托贷款 802 亿元，同比多增 519 亿元。一方面，可能与新
型政策性金融工具开始投放有关。部分资金通过股东借款的形式投入项
目。相关基金委托政策性银行向项目公司股东提供借款，带动委托贷款增
加；另一方面，公积金贷款需求边际改善亦对委托贷款形成一定支撑。未
贴现银行承兑汇票同比少增。9 月未贴现银行承兑汇票增加 2153 亿元，
同比少增 1081 亿元，主要与季末银行加大收票力度，票据被贴现至表内
有关。 
M1 增速回落。9 月 M1 同比增长 3.2%，较上月回落 0.9 个百分点。或主
要受 9 月资本市场调整、市场风险偏好边际回落影响，资金活化程度有所
减弱。9 月非银存款亦减少 1.9 万亿元，同比多减 8500 亿元。 
M2 增速小幅回落。9 月 M2 同比增长 7.2%，较上月回落 0.3 个百分点。
一是信贷派生仍然偏弱，新增人民币贷款同比少增，银行体系通过信贷 派
生存款的能力受限；二是财政存款同比少减 4100 亿元，财政资金向企业
和居民存款转化力度偏弱，亦对 M2 形成一定拖累。 
政策加力有望支撑四季度社融企稳 。往前看，一是货币端，PSL 利率下调
至 1.5%，支持范围扩展至“六张网”，叠加科技创新和技术改造再贷
款、支农支小再贷款额度增加，有助于降低重点领域融资成本、拓宽重大
项目和薄弱环节的融资渠道，为后续信贷投放提供支撑 ；二是准财政端，
8000 亿元新型政策性金融工具已启动投放，项目资本金补充有望撬动配
套贷款及后续债券融资；三是财政端，5500 亿元地方政府债务结存限额
已安排使用，其中 2500 亿元专项债重点支持在建项目和“六张网”等领
域。叠加四季度政府债发行提速，相关政策效果有望逐步显现，对社融企
稳形成有效支撑。

风险提示：宏观经济不及预期，地产销售不及预期，美国货币政策超预
期，地缘政治风险超预期。

◼ 3Q26 컨센서스 부합, 4Q26 성장폭 확대 전망 
◼ 5공장 Ramp-up 지속, 신규 수주 회복 여부에 주목 
◼ 투자의견 매수, 목표주가 2,000,000원 유지

투자의견 매수, 목표주가 2,000,000원 유지 
목표주가는 DCF 방식으로 산출(WACC 8.1%, 영구성장률 3.5% 적용)

3Q26 Preview: 단기 부담에도 컨센서스 부합 전망 
3Q26 매출액 1조 3,380억원(+6.4% yoy, +1.3% qoq), 영업이익 5,890억원(-
7.0% yoy, +0.4% qoq, OPM 44.0%)으로 컨센서스에 부합하는 실적을 전
망함. 3분기 평균 원/달러 환율이 전분기 대비 약 5% 하락하고, 지난 5월 
파업에 따른 생산 차질 영향이 반영되나, 5공장 PPQ 물량의 매출 인식이 
시작되며 yoy, qoq 외형 성장은 이어질 것으로 예상함.  
파업 당시 생산이 지연된 물량은 3Q 중 추가 생산되어 4Q26부터 매출에 반
영될 전망임. 5공장은 다수 프로젝트의 PPQ가 진행 중이며, Ramp-up에 
따라 매출 기여도 점진적으로 확대될 것으로 예상함. 이에 4Q26에는 파
업 이연 물량 반영과 5공장 매출 확대에 따라 실적 성장폭이 확대될 전
망임. 2026년 연간 매출액과 영업이익은 각각 5조 5,310억원(+21.4% 
yoy), 2조 4,780억원(+19.8% yoy, OPM 44.8%)으로 전망함.

신규 수주 회복 기대, 중장기 성장 기반은 확대 중 
관세 관련 불확실성으로 둔화됐던 고객사와의 수주 논의가 재개되고 있음. 
10월 2일 유럽 소재 제약사와 935억원 규모의 CMO 계약을 추가 체결
했으며, 4Q26 신규 수주 회복 여부에 주목함.  
PolyPeptide 인수는 연내 거래 종결을 목표로 진행 중이며, 인수 완료 
시 펩타이드 분야로 CDMO 포트폴리오가 확대될 전망임. 6 공장도 연내 
착공을 목표로 준비 중임. 
한편, 노사 리스크는 단기 불확실성 요인임. 노조는 10/26부터 3주간 2
차 총파업을 예고함. 1차 대비 예정된 파업 기간이 길어진 만큼 실제 파
업 진행 시 생산 차질 규모가 주요 변수임. 다만 파업 개시 전까지 협상 
가능성이 열려 있어, 향후 노사 협상 진행 경과를 확인할 필요가 있음.

Underperform(매도) 
비율 93.2% 6.8% 0.0% 
산업 투자의견 
- Overweight(비중확대) 
  : 향후 6개월간 업종지수상승률이 시장수익률 대비 초과 상승 예상 
- Neutral(중립) 
  : 향후 6개월간 업종지수상승률이 시장수익률과 유사한 수준 예상 
- Underweight(비중축소) 
  : 향후 6개월간 업종지수상승률이 시장수익률 대비 하회 예상

기업 투자의견 
- Buy(매수)  
  : 향후 6개월간 시장수익률 대비 10%p 이상 주가 상승 예상 
- Marketperform(시장수익률) 
  : 향후 6개월간 시장수익률 대비 -10%p~10%p 주가 변동 예상 
- Underperform(시장수익률 하회)

Editorial 
A Fed credibility-induced bounce  
Strategy summary:  The Fed’s unanimous decision to hike 
rates this week for the first time since 2023 , and pencil in 
another hike by December, confirms our expectations – flagged 
in the last two weeklies - of a central bank willing to establish its 
independence against political pressure. The Fed’s moderately 
hawkish pivot is helping restore its inflation-fighting credentials. 
The BoJ also hiked rates this week. It is the first time on record 
all three major central banks – Fed, ECB and BoJ – have hiked 
in the same month , as they respond primarily to a resurgence 
in energy-driven inflation amid tight job markets.  
We expect the Fed to hike once more by year-end to 4.0-4.25% 
and follow up with another hike in H1 2027 to 4.25 -4.5%. With 
the revised estimates, we revise up our US government bond 
yield forecasts. Nevertheless, with Fed policy uncertainty 
easing, we see an opportunity to use the equity market dip to 
add exposure amid solid corporate earnings.  
Fed moves to restore credibility:  The Fed’s policy pivot this 
week followed Chair Warsh’s hawkish Jackson Hole speech 
and recent economic data prints, namely: a pick -up in job 
creation and inflation over the summer and a sharp rebound in 
oil prices due to the re -escalation of the Middle E ast conflict. 
Given the unanimous Fed decision to hike rates this week, we 
expect two more hikes by  June 2027 on the back of  robust 
growth amid strong AI investments and a tight labour market, 
keeping inflation elevated. We expect this pivot to help the Fed 
rebuild credibility as an inflation fighter.  
Policy outlook less aggressive than market pricing : The 
updated Fed policy rate estimates are lower compared with 
money market pricing of three more rate hikes by June 2027 
since we expect disinflation to take hold by Q2 2027 as the 
impact of oil prices and tariffs fade. We believe policy rates can 
return to the 4.0–4.25% range by end-2027, with the possibility 
of cuts in H2 2027 as inflation pressures ease.  
Upgrading near-term US rates and USD estimates : Based 
on these revised estimates, we also upgrade our 3-month target 
for the US 10 -year government bond yield to 5.0 -5.25%. We 
also revise up our 12 -month target for the US 10 -year bond 
yield to 4.75-5.0% (see page 4).  
Buying the equity dip as focus turns to fundamentals: Fed 
hikes typically do not hurt equities over the medium-term when 
accompanied by strong earnings growth. We estimate a 25bps 
rise in Fed rates (the discount rate) would impact the S&P500 
index and global equities by 3 -4%. However, we expect the 
headwind from higher rates to be offset by strong earnings 
growth, driving positive performance for global equities.  
Furthermore, a hawkish Fed is largely priced in and we expect 
the Fed to deliver fewer rate hikes than currently priced by 
markets. Meanwhile, we believe concerns over AI industry 
leaders slowing the pace of development are overdone. Hence, 
we maintain our preference for a diversified group of large cap 
technology and semiconductor industry leaders (see page 5-6). 
BoJ hikes; modestly bullish JPY : The BoJ hiked rates by 
25bps to a three-decade high of 1.25%, as expected, but the 7-
2 split vote led to renewed JPY weakness on concerns it may 
slow the pace of further hikes. We remain constructive on JPY 
amid narrowing rate differential vs peers as the BoJ potentially 
hikes rates by 25bps once per quarter until Q2 2027 to curb 
rising domestic wage-driven inflation pressure . Any sign of BoJ 
slowing the pace of hikes could lead to renewed JPY weakness, 
fuelling inflationary pressures (see page 7).  
BoE holds; rangebound GBP: The BoE held rates this week, 
bucking the hiking trend among peers, as weakening UK job 
and housing markets offset rebound in energy-driven inflation. 
We expect BoE to deliver fewer rate hikes than the 100bps of 
increase currently priced by markets  as the job market slows 
further. This is likely to keep GBP rangebound (see page 7).  
—  Rajat Bhattacharya

### Page 4

Standard Chartered Bank 
WS Global CIO Office | 18 September 2026

Top client questions 
  Where do you see US government bond  yields following 
the Fed’s 16 September decision to hike interest rates? 
Our view: The US 10-year government bond yield is likely to 
retest the 5.00-5.25% range in the near term. We favour 3-7-year 
maturity bond s in t he US, preferring credit over government 
bonds. We are looking for an opportunity to extend duration 
approaching Q2 2027. 
Rationale: Over the next few months, we expect 2- and 30-year US 
government bond yields to remain rangebound, but the 10-year yield 
is expected to rebound up to the 5.00-5.25% range. The reasons for 
this include: 1) oil prices staying elevated and potentially rise further 
due to the fluid geopolitical situation in the Middle East, with Saudi 
Arabia’s East-West oil pipeline shut, the Houthis now firmly in control 
of the Strait of Bab-al-Mandab, the Strait of Hormuz still closed and 
China returning as a buyer of crude oil in the market ; 2) despite 
notable AI leaders expressing the need to slow AI development, AI 
capex plans are showing no signs of slowing down, which continues 
to drive above-trend growth in the economy. This, in turn, also means 
AI-related heavy bond issuance  is expected to continue. These 
issuers typically issue long-dated tenors, and this competition for 
capital will also exert upward pressure on the US 10-year yields. 
We note that the US 10-year bond yield has room to move lower, 
driven by short-covering activities in the very near term as net-short 
positions had moved to extreme levels before th is week’s  Fed 
meeting. However, we think such downshifts would be short-lived 
and the central tendency for the 10-year yield would be to move back 
up to the 5.00-5.25% range. 
Meanwhile, we see US 30-year yields continuing to reflect the dire 
fiscal outlook for the US government. We expect US 2-year yields 
to continue trading in a range that reflects expectations of further 
federal funds rate hikes. For the record, we only expect two more 
rate hikes from the Fed into the middle of 2027 , while money 
markets are pricing in three more for the same period. 
Going into H2 2027, we expect oil prices to moderate (either 
through a resolution of the M iddle East conflict or a re-routing of oil 
supply chains)  and the inflationary effects from tariffs to fade. 
Inflation by then is likely be close to – if not at – the Fed’s target of 
2%. At this point, we see room for the Fed to contemplate rate 
cuts. US 2-year yields (the tenor that is most sensitive to Fed policy 
changes) will likely start to price th is in. With oil prices and inflation 
cooling, 10-year yields would also have room to come down. 
US 30-year yields, on the other hand, are expected to  remain 
elevated as the US fiscal deficit remains substantial next year and 
could become much worse than its already dire state today. As a 
result, the ‘term premium ’ may keep the ultra -long end elevated. 
Short of some unorthodox policy to artificially cap them lower, 30 -
year yields could continue to drift higher into H2 2027. 
We continue to favour the 3-7-year segment of the yield curve and 
prefer credit over government bond s. We see an opportunity for 
us to extend duration closer to Q2 2027. 
—  Jonathan Liang, CFA, CIO for Fixed Income and FX

for US government bond yields

### Page 5

Standard Chartered Bank 
WS Global CIO Office | 18 September 2026

Top client questions (cont’d) 
  Will the step -up in the  Fed’s risk-free rate trigger a re -
pricing of equities? 
Our view: We expect strong corporate earnings growth to offset 
the headwind of higher rates , driving positive performance for 
global equities. We remain Overweight global equities. 
Rationale: The Fed’s latest 25bps rate hike had been anticipated 
by the bond market, partly reflected in the bond yield  rise we have 
seen so far in 2026. Using the US 10-year government bond yield as 
a proxy for the risk -free rate, the yield has risen by 80-85bps this 
year. We estimate that each 25bps rise in the discount rate presents 
a valuation drag on global equities. On this basis, the rise in yields 
represents a potential 10-14% headwind for equities this year. 
However, the Fed’s hike reflects a solid expansion of US economic 
activity, resilient domestic spending and robust capital investment. 
This is consistent with the positive earnings revisions we have seen 
this year, with  2026 earnings growth projections now at 34% (and 
16% for 2027). Global equities are up 14% year-to-date (YTD), but 
even with a 14% valuation headwind from higher yields, we believe 
they can rise further, given strong earnings growth in 2026 and 2027. 
—  Fook Hien Yap, Senior Investment Strategist 
  What is the outlook for the US 10-year government bond 
yield? What is your tactical view on the DXY? 
Our view: We raise our three-month US 10 -year government 
bond yield forecast to the 5.00-5.25% range. Meanwhile, we see 
a near-term upward bias in the DXY after its recent downtrend, 
supported by recent Fed tightening and higher  short-term US 
bond yields.  
Rationale: The Fed 's decision  to raise the target range for the 
federal funds rate by 25bps to 3.75-4.00% validates the upside policy 
risks we flagged recently, following Fed Chair Warsh's Jackson Hole 
speech and a run of firmer US economic data. We expect growth 
to remain robust, the labour market  to remain tight and inflation to 
stay elevated, supporting one more hike before year-end 2026 and 
potentially another in H1 2027  – fewer than the three additional 
hikes currently priced in by money markets . For the US 10-year 
government bond yield, further policy tightening and lingering US 
fiscal concerns should keep yields elevated, but the Fed’s visible 
response to inflation should limit additional upward pressure, leaving 
the 10-year yield holding around the 5.00-5.25% range. 
Crucially, Warsh provided no forward guidance on further rate 
hikes. Markets had already priced in substantial additional rate hikes 
ahead of the Fed meeting, reducing the scope for further USD upside 
purely from rate repricing. Meanwhile, we expect further policy 
tightening from the ECB and the BoJ, while the RBA also remains 
biased towards further tightening. This should gradually narrow the 
US rate advantage and limit the extent of a DXY rebound. 
—  Vincent Tan, Senior Investment Strategist 
—  Anthony Naab, CFA, Investment Strategist

Global equities’ earnings growth estimates have 
been revised higher this year, offsetting the 
headwind from higher rates

### Page 6

Standard Chartered Bank 
WS Global CIO Office | 18 September 2026

Top client questions (cont’d) 
  How should investors position in technology as AI lab 
CEOs call for a slowdown in frontier AI model development? 
Our view: We believe concerns over “pacing the frontier” are 
overdone. We maintain our preference to remain invested in 
and diversified across Big Tech and semiconductor leaders. 
Rationale: We believe it is prudent for investors to avoid 
overreacting to the recent calls for a slowdown in frontier AI model 
development, as they focus on responsible AI development, rather 
than halting AI model development or reducing spending. The global 
AI leadership  race, reinforced by the US and China’s  strategic 
ambitions, also makes a coordinated slowdown unlikely. 
While the near-term narrative may shift from AI training beneficiaries 
towards inference plays, hyperscalers continue to signal strong AI 
infrastructure commitments and improving monetisation, supporting 
our AI capex outlook. Rising AI adoption and agentic use cases 
should sustain inference demand, even if training activity moderates, 
benefiting custom AI chips, NAND flash memory and hyperscalers.  
We believe the decline in valuations  this year more than 
compensates for any downside risk to AI capex. Our Opportunistic 
idea on Global Semiconductors further reflects our conviction, with 
attractive valuations supporting selective additions during volatility. 
—  Ryan Goh, Investment Strategist 
  With ASEAN and UK equities outperforming AI-focused 
markets in H2 2026, is it worth adding to these markets? 
Our view: ASEAN and UK equities offer portfolio diversification, 
but we are Underweight both markets in favour of US and Asia 
ex-Japan (AxJ) equities, given their stronger earnings potential. 
Rationale: The ASEAN and UK markets have outperformed the 
MSCI ACWI over the past three months, but both have 
underperformed on a YTD basis. Financials is the largest sector in 
the ASEAN (53%) and the UK (28%) and provides steady earnings. 
However, both markets have minimal exposure to the technology 
sector (just 3% and 1% , respectively), ultimately dragging down 
growth. Hence, while defensive markets offer value by increasing 
diversification and reducing volatility in a portfolio, we maintain our 
Underweight allocation for ASEAN and the UK, as we see much 
stronger earnings potential elsewhere in the US and AxJ markets.  
We remain Overweight US and AxJ, supported by robust earnings 
growth, as  AI still has the potential to become a major economic 
growth driver . Our 12-month S&P500 Index forecast of 8,400  
implies low-teens upside, supported by a strong earnings backdrop 
of 25-30% earnings-per-share growth for 2026 and 15 -20% for 
2027. AxJ also boasts the strongest earnings growth among major 
markets, fuelled by the AI semiconductor super-cycle. 
—  Cindy Lam, CFA, Senior Investment Strategist

consensus expectations to account for execution

### Page 7

Standard Chartered Bank 
WS Global CIO Office | 18 September 2026

Top client questions (cont’d) 
  Why is it still appropriate to have a positive stance on US 
high-yield (HY) bonds? 
Our view: We remain opportunistically bullish on US HY bonds. 
Lower sensitivity to interest rate volatility, a below-trend default 
rate and a maturity wall pushed out to 2028-29 drive this view. 
Rationale: Recent interest rate volatility has highlighted a key 
structural advantage of US HY bonds: their higher coupon rates  
and shorter tenors relative to Developed Market (DM) government 
bonds and US investment-grade (IG) corporate bonds translate into 
a much lower sensitivity to interest rates, cushioning bond prices 
during yield backup episodes, where bond yields rise sharply. 
YTD, US HY has delivered 1.8% in total returns, outperforming DM 
government bonds and US IG corporate bonds by over 3% each on 
a relative basis. This outperformance was particularly evident during 
the recent back-up in bond yields, as inflation concerns re-emerged 
after renewed Middle East tensions – an environment where HY's 
lower rate sensitivity provided a relative cushion  even as broader 
fixed income markets came under pressure in absolute terms. On 
the fundamentals side, US HY corporate credit quality remains 
resilient. Default rates continue to track below long-run trend levels, 
and the maturity wall has been pushed out to 2028 -29, reducing 
near-term refinancing risk. Against a non -recessionary macro 
backdrop, we expect these credit fundamentals to hold, supporting 
the case for continued opportunistic exposure to the asset class.  
—  Ray Heung, Senior Investment Strategist 
  Do the latest BoJ and BoE meetings alter your view on the 
central banks’ rate paths and their corresponding currencies? 
Our view: We remain constructive on the JPY and expect two 
BoJ rate hikes this year and potentially one hike per quarter in 
H1 2027 as policy normalisation continues. Meanwhile, the BoE 
held rates at 3.75%. We expect GBP/USD to stay rangebound 
within 1.32-1.36 over the next 1-3 months, with downside risk 
towards the lower end of our medium-term 1.30-1.36 range. 
Rationale: Persistent inflation, higher energy prices and earlier JPY 
weakness support further BoJ policy normalisation . Continued 
hiking should progressively narrow the US -Japan rate gap, reduce 
the attractiveness of JPY -funded carry trades and support  the JPY 
over the medium term towards the 153-155 range. However, with a 
September hike largely priced  in, future tightening ’s pace  will be 
more important for the JPY. Meanwhile, the BoE was split 6-3 in its 
decision to hold rates at 3.75% , with three members preferring a 
25bps hike, signalling elevated inflation risks. However, a weaker 
labour market is likely to limit the pace of BoE tightening. The US-
UK rate divergence is expected to favour the USD over the next 1-3 
months. Further BoE tightening (if inflation remains elevated ) could 
narrow the rate gap and provide support to the GBP later in the cycle. 
—  Vincent Tan, Senior Investment Strategist

We expect US HY bonds to continue delivering 
strong performance in the near term 
Total returns: US HY corporate, US IG corporate and 
DM government bond indices

Source: Bloomberg, Standard Chartered

## Document 232: Advanced economies _ Resilience to shock after shock (2026-09-24)

Source PDF: all report pdf/Advanced economies  _ Resilience to shock after shock (2026-09-24).pdf
Report source: BNP Paribas
Date: 2026-09-24
Pages: 3
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

3
economic-research.bnpparibas.comEcoPerspectives / 3rd Quarter 2026 / September 2026
The bank
for a changing
world
History tends to repeat itself in advanced economies. Once again, growth ultimately fell short of expectations by only a small
margin in the first half of 2026, despite the conflict in Iran. As early as 2025, the impact of tariffs was less severe than feared.
This is a sign that structural factors (notably AI, defence and electrification) are underpinning growth in these countries

and are expected to continue to do so in 2027. However, there are significant risks to the economic outlook, primarily of a
geopolitical nature, with a significant upside risk to inflation and interest rates. The other instance of history repeating itself
concerns a period that those under 20 cannot have experienced: the period leading up to the 2008 crisis, echoes of which can
be seen in terms of growth, inflation, the direction of monetary policy, and long-term interest rates.

Advanced economies are experiencing trends similar to those they
faced nearly 20 years ago:
- Growth remains resilient in 2025-26 despite shocks, as it did in 2006-
07, in the major advanced economies.
- Inflation is above central banks’ targets: as in 2006-07, this calls for a
tightening of monetary policy.
- In Europe and the United States, long-term interest rates have re -
turned to their 2006-07 levels (1996 in Japan): a challenging environ-

ment for public finances in a context where public debt ratios are signi-
ficantly higher than those seen 20 years ago.
THE GLOBAL ECONOMY IN THE MIDST OF A GROWTH CYCLE
We are already well into 2026, and the picture so far is clear: glo -
bal growth is proving resilient. Following a US tariff shock last year,
growth is once again proving, for the time being, to be significantly less
affected by the conflict in Iran than had been anticipated when it first

broke out. Initially, however, there were fears that this new shock would
hit growth even harder. As with the tariff hike in 2025, the impact was
measurable, particularly in terms of consumer confidence. But in both
2025 and 2026, growth has also shown a degree of resilience.
The figures for the second quarter bear this out, despite the sharp rise
in oil prices, the recessionary impact of which is not yet apparent.
According to our forecasts, growth was expected to reach or exceed 1%

in 2026 in the four major advanced economies (the Eurozone, Japan,
the United Kingdom and the United States, where it exceeds 2%) for
the second consecutive year. This had not happened (excluding the
post-COVID catch-up period) since 2006-07. Growth figures are more
moderate (particularly for demographic reasons), but the private sec -
tor is not (at present) showing the same excesses (at that time, the
credit boom had preceded the worst crisis since the 1930s); although

debt levels have recently risen in the US tech sector.
The driving force behind the current growth cycle is clear: artificial
intelligence (AI). It is fuelling global trade and business investment
in most countries. This is clearly the case in the United States, where
business investment is growing strongly as investment plans in the
US tech sector are implemented.
However, the world’s major economies are currently engaged in a
broader push to expand their capital goods investment. Alongside the

ongoing development of services—particularly digital services—we
have seen a rebound in industrial production in most countries since
early 2025. The leading sectors are primarily technology equipment,
machinery, aeronautics, defence and their essential inputs (energy and
metals). This trend is evident everywhere, including in Europe, and
is becoming entrenched over the long term, with an improvement in
business climate indicators, particularly new export orders. Growth is

therefore expected to strengthen further in 2027, particularly in the
Eurozone, where German investment plans are set to gather momen -
tum and whose spillover effects will have a greater impact on the eco-
nomy.
However, not all sectors are experiencing the same momentum. This
is particularly true for the sectors that depend on household consump-
tion, which has shown mixed trends. Our observation that consumer
spending is more buoyant in the United States than in Europe was

confirmed in 2026, and the outlook does not point to a change in this
ranking.
EDITORIAL
RESILIENCE TO SHOCK AFTER SHOCK

### Page 2

4
EcoPerspectives / 3rd Quarter 2026 / September 2026 economic-research.bnpparibas.com
The bank
for a changing
world
While European and US households are experiencing more or less the
same energy price shock, inflation remains higher in the United States.
But this has not prevented consumer spending from keeping pace, pri-
marily because it is driven by the wealthiest households, who are less
sensitive to inflation than to the wealth effect. Furthermore, while the

savings rate remains high in the Eurozone, US households have been
drawing down their savings. This is also true of British households,
which explains why the UK has seen slightly stronger growth since
the beginning of the year. In Japan, government spending has partially
offset energy inflation (while other countries are doing so to a much
lesser extent than in 2022), which has supported consumption growth.
However, these buffers—reduced savings or fiscal support—have their

limits when shocks persist. The two major current conflicts (Iran and
Ukraine) are far from being resolved, and their impact is continuing.
This persistent geopolitical risk and its implications for inflation repre-
sent a significant headwind to the growth outlook for 2027.
HIGHER INFLATION CALLS FOR MONETARY TIGHTENING
Inflation is exceeding the target set by three of the four central banks
examined in this publication, which is another similarity (albeit with

different underlying causes) with the 2006-07 period. Japan is the ex -
ception, as government support measures for households are tempo -
rarily holding back inflation.
Apart from the Bank of Japan, which is engaged in a long-term norma-
lisation process, no central bank was expected to raise its key rates
in 2026. But they are now being prompted to do so for reasons that
vary from one region to another. The first is the conflict in Iran and
the resulting rise in oil prices, which triggered a rebound in energy

inflation from the second quarter onwards. However, since the conflict
was not resolved by the memorandum of understanding signed in June,
and given that the subsequent drop in oil prices proved short-lived,
inflation is expected to rise again. The rise in gas prices is expected to
exacerbate the impact, particularly in Europe, where inventories are
being replenished ahead of winter. Electricity prices are expected to
follow suit in most countries, but at a more moderate pace than in

2022. Indeed, gas is now used less to generate electricity than it was
at that time (thanks to the expansion of renewable energy), and to an
even lesser extent in France.
There is a significant risk that energy inflation will spread further to
other components of inflation in the future, and inflation expectations
could eventually reflect this. This is why the European Central Bank
has already raised its key interest rate twice (in June and September)

and, according to our scenario, will likely do so again in December. For
the same reason, we expect the Bank of England to raise its policy rate
in November. Although the cap on energy prices was maintained for
a long time, it has now been raised. As a result, inflation is expected
to rise between now and the end of the year. The aim will then be to
prevent second-round effects.
Inflation can also be a sign of (too much) economic strength. In the

United States, it has exceeded the 2% target since 2021. The main
argument in favour of a new cycle of rate hikes (following the one in
2022-2023) lies in the current resilience of growth and the improving
labour market. The Federal Reserve (Fed) must balance two equally
important objectives: maximum employment and price stability. Howe-
ver, unlike in the 4 th quarter of 2025, when the decline in job crea -
tion justified monetary easing (by 75 basis points), the current sharp

rebound in job creation completely reverses that analysis. According
to our forecasts, the unemployment rate is expected to decrease in
the coming months, which pushes back the prospect of a decline in
core inflation. This situation persists due to both supply constraints
(over which a central bank has no control) and excess demand (which
the Fed can, however, mitigate). Consequently, the Fed raised its key
rate in September and is expected to implement two further rate hikes

between now and January 2027.
In Japan, finally, while headline inflation has been brought down by
government support measures, core inflation remains significant.
Wage pressures are mounting against a backdrop of labour shortages.
The Bank of Japan is therefore being forced to accelerate the pace of its
monetary normalisation.
In all these regions, inflation is currently expected to peak in Q4 2026
or Q1 2027. It is against this backdrop of temporary inflation that rate

hikes could also come to an end by that time (excluding Japan). Howe-
ver, this assumes that once the energy shock—the course of which
remains uncertain—subsides, inflation will fall. The effects of El Niño,
which are still difficult to gauge at present, could take over. Inflation
would then remain above the central banks’ target for a longer period.
Under this scenario, rate hikes would go further than is currently en -
visaged.
THE RISE IN LONG- TERM INTEREST RATES: A FACTOR THAT WILL WEIGH

ON PUBLIC FINANCES
Monetary policies have also to offset the inflationary effects of fiscal
policy, particularly in the United States (where the budget deficit is
high and shows no signs of shrinking) and in Japan (where the new
government is pursuing pro-growth policies). In Europe, fiscal policy
is playing a much smaller role than it did in 2022. While the stimulus
measures implemented at that time did help reduce the inflation rate
in the short term (by nearly 2.5 pp in France, for example), by keeping

demand at a high level, they delayed disinflation and increased the
scale of the monetary tightening required.
The outbreak of the conflict in Iran acted as a catalyst: it triggered a
rise in long-term rates (which had been trending downwards at the
start of the year) to levels close to those of 2006-07. This rise is wides-
pread, but its causes vary. Inflation expectations appear to play a more
significant role in the Eurozone, whereas the rise in real interest rates

seems to be the main factor in the United States and Japan.
SOURCE: LSEG, BNP PARIBASCHART 3
Consumer Price Inflation (y/y)

### Page 3

5
EcoPerspectives / 3rd Quarter 2026 / September 2026 economic-research.bnpparibas.com
The bank
for a changing
world
Higher inflation and more resilient real growth than feared point in
the same direction: stronger than expected nominal growth that is li -
kely to be sustained. However, in the long term, high nominal growth
tends to drive up nominal interest rates (as it is a positive sign of more
profitable investment projects), in contrast to the problem of secular

stagnation raised in the 2010s (which combined low nominal growth
with low nominal interest rates).
The other issue is the high level of fiscal deficits (too high for a period
of growth), with various situations. In several countries, the public
debt-to-GDP ratio continues to rise. In the United States, deficit reduc-
tion does not appear to be a priority, to the extent that it is expected
to remain above 6% of GDP at the very least until the end of Donald

Trump’s term of office (with little hope that the mid-term elections will
significantly alter this trend). In the United Kingdom and France, fiscal
consolidation has begun, but it is being hampered by the simultaneous
increase in certain areas of expenditure (debt servicing, defence and
healthcare, due in particular to an ageing population), which is slowing
the pace of consolidation. In Germany, the implementation of invest -
ment plans is leading to a widening of the deficit. While this does not

pose a sustainability problem, it does result in an increase in annual
debt issuance. In Japan, the budget deficit is kept in check by interest
payments that remain low, as the rise in interest rates is recent and
the average maturity of the debt is high (10 years). However, expansio-
nary fiscal policy could pose further problems in terms of debt dyna -
mics if it were to continue through the end of the decade. In Italy and
Spain, fiscal consolidation has been sufficient to stabilise the public

debt-to-GDP ratio (and even to reduce it in Spain), but the high level of
Italy’s debt service it vulnerable to a rise in interest rates.
In all these countries, the fiscal arithmetic is simpler today than it
will be in 2030. Indeed, in most cases, only half of the debt was is -
sued after 2022 (that is, after the end of the zero-interest-rate period).
The debt service has so far only partially risen, except in the United
States, where most of the increase has already occurred due to a shor-

ter average debt maturity (6 years). This means that, for all countries
combined, the rate of GDP growth remains higher than the average
interest rate paid on public debt (or the effective interest rate).
By 2030, the effective rate will have risen and caught up with
GDP growth. As a result, in most cases, the debt service will have re -
turned to the level as a share of GDP seen in the early 2000s. In order to
stabilise public debt-to-GDP ratios, primary balances will need to have

improved. This will even require the generation of primary surpluses,
particularly in Italy (which is already achieving this), France and the
United Kingdom. This is a crucial shift that governments will need to
introduce as early as the 2027 budget; otherwise, they risk postponing
the stabilisation of their public debt-to-GDP ratio once again.
Article completed on 17 September 2026
Stéphane Colliac
stephane.colliac@bnpparibas.com

## Document 317: Eurozone_ Improving business sentiment, driven by tech, is spreading to other sectors (2026-10-14)

Source PDF: all report pdf/Eurozone_ Improving business sentiment, driven by tech, is spreading to other sectors (2026-10-14).pdf
Report source: BNP Paribas
Date: 2026-10-14
Pages: 3
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

14 October 2026
The bank
for a changing
world
EUROZONE: IMPROVING BUSINESS SENTIMENT, DRIVEN BY TECH, IS SPREADING TO OTHER SECTORS
Stéphane Colliac & Tarik Rharrab
*LATEST OBSERVATION: 5 OCTOBER 2026  **ESTIMATE
The September PMI surveys confirm that growth in the Eurozone is getting stronger, boosted in particular by new export orders, which are
still rising. Sectors that were already performing well – technology and capital goods (including for defence) – are continuing to grow, while

those that had been struggling until now – aeronautics, consumer goods and financial services – are seeing their production indices recover.
This strong performance is particularly noteworthy, given that it has not yet been undermined by higher inflation.
The heatmap shows the PMI index readings: a reading above 50 indicates expansion, while a reading below 50 signals contraction compared to the previous
month. For each indicator, sectors are ranked in descending order based on their September 2026 reading.

Shades of red represent values above 50, while shades of blue represent values below 50: the intensity of the colour increases the further away they are
from this threshold.
Production growth spreads to more sectors
The PMI surveys on business sentiment confirm that business activity in the Eurozone is expanding at a faster pace and spreading to more sectors.
Household-consumption-related sectors (goods and services), which were adversely affected in the spring by the outbreak of the conflict in the Middle

East, do not appear to be negatively impacted by the rising inflation observed since August. The aeronautics sector, where production has mainly been
limited by supply constraints, is also showing signs of improvement.

These trends, although still moderate, come alongside a drive to upgrade equipment, fuelled by investment in digital technology, electrification
(which is supporting the automotive sector) and defence. The momentum in technology goods and services further strengthened in September.
The production index for computer and electronics hit a five-year high, while the software services production index stayed close to its July peak.

Financial services are also enjoying a significant improvement, reflecting the healthy lending momentum despite interest rates starting to rise
(see our analysis).
Growth driven by exports
Exports drove growth in the first half of 2026, both across the Eurozone as a whole (see our analysis) and among its key members (notably
Germany). This momentum is also increasing, as indicated by the ‘new export orders’ component of the various PMI indices. As was the case with

production, there has been a particularly noteworthy increase for capital goods (technology and machinery) and related services, particularly digital
services. This trend is playing out more broadly, as, in September, new export orders rose across all sub-sectors, with the exception of intermediate
goods and consumer goods, where contraction recently eased. There has been a significant improvement in the transport sector, particularly in
the aeronautics industry, which has major markets in Asia. Recent data on French exports show that aeronautics exports to this region are rising

once again.
The convergence between rising production and increasing export-order backlogs shows that Eurozone growth is based on an increasingly solid
foundation. Inflationary pressures are not holding it back for the time being.
stephane.colliac@bnpparibas.com
tarik.rharrab@bnpparibas.com
With the help of Ece Usta (intern).

## Document 320: Fed tăng lãi suất, dự báo thêm một lần tăng nữa trong năm (2026-09-17)

Source PDF: all report pdf/Fed tăng lãi suất, dự báo thêm một lần tăng nữa trong năm (2026-09-17).pdf
Report source: KBSV (Thematic)
Date: 2026-09-17
Pages: 3
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

1

F I R S T  T O  M A R K E T

Fed tăng lãi suất, dự báo thêm 
một lần tăng nữa trong năm 
Đánh giá áp lực lên tỷ giá và lãi suất Việt Nam

Fed tăng lãi suất và dự báo có thêm một lần tăng lãi suất nữa trong năm 
— Trong kỳ họp FOMC ngày 15–16/09/2026, Fed quyết định tăng 25bps lãi suất điều hành 
lên mức 3.75%–4%, đánh dấu lần tăng lãi suất đầu tiên kể từ năm 2023. Quyết định 
được thông qua với tỷ lệ đồng thuận 12–0, phản ánh sự thống nhất của các thành viên 
FOMC trong việc ưu tiên kiểm soát lạm phát. 
— Chủ tịch Fed Kevin Warsh nhấn mạnh lạm phát vẫn ở mức cao và cần được đưa về mục 
tiêu 2% trong thời gian phù hợp hơn. Bên cạnh đó, Fed đánh giá hoạt động kinh tế 
đang tăng trưởng vững, tiêu dùng nội địa duy trì tích cực, năng suất và đầu tư vốn tăng 
trưởng mạnh, trong khi thị trường lao động vẫn ổn định. Điều này cho thấy Fed chưa 
nhận thấy áp lực suy giảm kinh tế đủ lớn để ưu tiên nới lỏng chính sách tiền tệ.  Ông 
Warsh không đưa ra cam kết cụ thể về thời điểm tăng lãi suất, nhấn mạnh chính sách 
sẽ phụ thuộc vào diễn biến lạm phát, thị trường lao động và các dữ liệu kinh tế sắp tới. 
— Biểu đồ dot plot tháng 9 cho thấy Fed vẫn nghiêng về khả năng tăng thêm lãi suất 
trong năm 2026, với 16/18 thành viên dự báo ít nhất một lần tăng nữa, đưa lãi suất điều 
hành lên vùng 4%–4.25%. Tuy nhiên, sự phân hóa trong dự báo lãi suất năm 2027 
(3.625%–4.375%) cho thấy đây có thể là một lần điều chỉnh nhằm kiểm soát lạm phát, 
thay vì tín hiệu về một chu kỳ thắt chặt kéo dài.

Quan điểm của KBSV đối với lãi suất và tỷ giá 
— Trong 3 tháng cuối năm, chúng tôi đánh giá nhiều khả năng Fed sẽ có thêm một lần 
tăng lãi suất 25bps, dựa trên:  
(1) Fed tiếp tục nâng dự báo PCE headline và core 2026 lên thêm 10 bps so với dự 
báo hồi tháng 6, đồng thời dự báo lạm phát sẽ quay về mức mục tiêu 2% vào năm 
2029 (thay vì năm 2028 như dự báo trước đó), trong khi dự báo về tỷ lệ thất 
nghiệp và tăng trưởng GDP được điều chỉnh theo hướng tích cực hơn. 
(2) Giá dầu vẫn duy trì ở mức cao (dự kiến quay về vùng 9x USD/thùng vào quý 4) 
trong bối cảnh xung đột tại Trung Đông tiếp tục gây gián đoạn hoạt động vận 
chuyển và xuất khẩu dầu mỏ. Mặc dù Saudi Arabia đã tăng cường vận chuyển 
dầu qua Oman, qua đó giảm bớt lo ngại về thiếu hụt nguồn cung trong ngắn hạn, 
rủi ro gián đoạn vẫn hiện hữu khi xung đột chưa được giải quyết hoàn toàn. 
— Chúng tôi nâng dự báo DXY lên vùng 98–103 điểm trong phần còn lại của năm với động 
lực hỗ trợ USD trong ngắn hạn đến từ việc Fed phát tín hiệu tiếp tục thắt chặt, trong khi 
thị trường điều chỉnh kỳ vọng lãi suất theo hướng duy trì cao hơn. Tuy nhiên, việc các 
NHTW lớn khác cũng duy trì định hướng kiểm soát lạm phát có thể hạn chế mức độ mở 
rộng chênh lệch lãi suất, qua đó làm giảm khả năng USD tăng giá một chiều và DXY 
tăng mạnh khỏi vùng dự báo.

### Page 2

— Chúng tôi dự báo vĩ mô trong nước đối với: (1) tỷ giá USD/VND tới cuối năm 2026 đi 
ngang so với cuối 2025 nhờ nguồn cung USD tích cực (từ kiều hối, FDI giải ngân và nâng 
hạng thị trường vốn) và chênh lệch lãi suất VND-USD dù có xu hướng thu hẹp nhưng 
vẫn duy trì trạng thái dương; và (2) lãi suất huy động sẽ duy trì ở vùng hiện tại trong 
phần còn lại của năm và kỳ vọng có thể giảm nhẹ nếu lạm phát được kiểm soát và 
NHNN sẽ có biện pháp hỗ trợ thanh khoản thị trường tích cực hơn.

## Document 326: Five ways the UK’s Autumn Budget could shock us

Source PDF: all report pdf/Five ways the UK’s Autumn Budget could shock us.pdf
Report source: ING Think
Date: 2026-10-16
Pages: 10
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

From better ﬁscal headroom to surprise income tax hikes, this
is how UK Chancellor John Healey's ﬁrst budget could surprise
investors
James Smith
UK Chancellor John Healey
In a nutshell: What we expect
Higher debt interest costs look set to drive the level of 'ﬁscal
headroom' from £24bn in March to £10bn. There's a growing
sense that Chancellor John Healey would like to get this up
to £15bn or higher. The government is set to modestly
increase spending on the cost of living, though the total

intervention is unlikely to meaningfully top £5bn and it's
likely to be mostly billed as temporary to avoid counting
against the main ﬁscal rule. We expect an increase in capital
spending of roughly £10bn/year, bearing in mind this is
exempt from the ﬁscal rule dictating that the current budget
must be in surplus after ﬁve years. To rebuild headroom

### Page 2

against that rule, we expect £5-6bn of tax hikes, likely via a
higher Bank Surcharge and possibly broadening the National
Insurance tax base.
In short, we don't expect the budget to deliver a material
stimulus (or add materially to the already-planned ﬁscal
consolidation). The impact on the Bank of England, which is
poised to hike rates in November, is likely to be fairly
minimal.
But all chancellors like to keep a rabbit in their hat – and what
follows are ﬁve ways it could surprise us.

1. Fiscal headroom goes up – not down
What if the UK’s ﬁscal hole isn’t nearly as bad as we think?
The widespread expectation going into the UK’s Autumn Budget on
28 October is that the Chancellor is going to have to face up to
higher debt interest costs with some modest tax hikes.
Bank of England rate expectations are almost a percentage point
higher than they were at the time of the Oﬃce for Budget
Responsibility’s March assessment of Britain’s public ﬁnances. 10-

year bond yields are some 70 basis points higher, which, taken
together, will push up debt interest spending by £11bn/year by
2029/30.
That points to a sizeable reduction in the amount of money the
government has left under its main ﬁscal rule, which requires the
current budget (day-to-day spending, including debt interest less
revenue) to be in surplus within three years. Back in March, that
surplus, or ﬁscal headroom, was projected at £24bn in 2029/30.

Debt interest costs are rising

*Based on the average rate in the 10 days up to 25 September, which

Source: Macrobond, ING
We think that headroom will have dropped to around £10bn before
any corrective action. And the vibe from the Treasury suggests the
Chancellor would prefer it to be above £15bn, to give more
breathing space in case of future adverse forecast revisions. That
would point to some limited tax hikes.
But what if that’s all wrong? The truth is, nobody really knows how

the OBR will change its assessment of the UK economy. And as we
saw last year, the Treasury is often inclined to talk up the doom and
gloom to open up a positive surprise on the day. 12 months ago,
the hit to headroom was considerably smaller than had been
widely expected.
This time, any positive surprise is most likely to come from wage
growth. Will the rise in energy prices spill into higher pay, just as it
did in 2022? We think it won’t, not least because the jobs market is

much weaker, an assessment we suspect the OBR will share.
But even relatively small upgrades to wage growth can make a big
diﬀerence to the government’s fortunes. Since 2010, the OBR has
revised the level of earnings across its ﬁve-year forecast by an
average of 1.3 percentage points in absolute terms. If it did that
now, say by lifting wage growth over the next two years by 0.6ppt
apiece, it would add £10bn to headroom, almost perfectly
oﬀsetting the hit from higher interest costs.

### Page 4

There are also reasons to think the impact of lower migration
projections, which some reports suggest could wipe another £7bn
oﬀ headroom via lower tax revenue forecasts, could be much less
than feared. The starting point for employment looks a little higher
than it did back in March. The rise in global equities this year should
also be helpful for capital gains tax projections.
Why does this all matter? Because the higher the headroom, the
less ‘need’ there is to hike taxes, and if it is signiﬁcantly higher than

expected, the more room there is to increase spending under the
ﬁscal rules.
The simple message is this: don’t judge the Autumn Budget by the
level of headroom left over.
2. Spending splurge
The problem with ‘headroom’ is not only that it’s highly sensitive to
the OBR’s economic outlook, but that the forward-looking nature of
the ﬁscal rules opens the door to a lot of creative accounting.
The impact of higher inﬂation is a case in point. This helps the

Treasury through higher receipts – both VAT and income tax. Yes, it
also pushes up the cost of indexed beneﬁts. But importantly,
departmental budgets don’t automatically increase to oﬀset the
impact of higher prices.
That opens up a classic trick from the Treasury, which is to leave
spending plans virtually unchanged in nominal terms, setting up
substantially smaller real-terms budget increases across the
medium term.
Even before the Iran War, government plans pencilled in real-terms

cuts to per capita departmental spending in FY2029. Given some
functions, like health, are ‘protected’, that implies much deeper cuts
to smaller departments.
Suﬃce it to say, that is unrealistic. Higher inﬂation will push up the
wage bill across government. It would cost £12bn to simply
maintain those March departmental spending plans in real terms.
That’s before considering the cost of ending that austerity pencilled

### Page 5

in for later this decade, or how the government will fund its defence
or social care ambitions.
A top-up to government departments is inevitable – the question is
when. It looks like the Treasury wants to kick the can on these tricky
budget decisions until next autumn, when it plans to release its
next Spending Review, a process of allocating money across
departments. No doubt the hope is that debt interest costs will
have fallen and the economic outlook brightened, such that there's

more space under the ﬁscal rules to make bigger spending
commitments.
That points to a very modest package of spending measures this
month, mainly targeting the cost of living. On top of the already-
announced cut to VAT on energy, we’d imagine the Treasury will
replicate last year’s budget and lower household bills by funding a
greater share of ‘policy costs’ from general taxation. This has the
added beneﬁt of lowering headline inﬂation at a particularly

sensitive moment for the Bank of England. Crucially though, the
government is likely to bill these moves as temporary, so they don’t
count under that main ﬁscal rule.
But if the economic picture really is better than feared, don’t rule
out the government going further. A decision to fully fund the UK’s
3% defence spending commitment would be a major wildcard,
though this would likely come at the expense of capital plans in
other areas of government.

Ultimately, the key thing to watch is the OBR’s assessment of how
all these policy changes aﬀect GDP growth and borrowing over the
next year. If there’s a material stimulus eﬀect, it could add to
market bets on UK rate hikes.
3. A manifesto break on income tax
Tax hikes are likely at the Autumn Budget, but should be very small.
We’re probably looking at a package of circa £5bn (0.2% of GDP).
Part of the problem is that the list of viable options looks perilously

small if Labour is to avoid breaking its manifesto pledge not to raise

The problem with many of these measures is that they require
massive changes to stand any chance of raising material sums,
risking big behavioural reactions while oﬀering uncertain revenue
potential. There’s a reason why former chancellor Rachel Reeves
shied away from many of the measures we outline in the chart

Is there scope for a surprise? We think there are two main ways on
tax – one good, one bad.
The good? A surprise announcement of a so-called ‘tax switch’, a
novel proposal from Resolution Foundation which would see the
government simultaneously raise basic income tax and cut

employee National Insurance by 2ppt. The impact on workers
would be neutral, but because income tax has a broader base
(including pensions), this would raise £6bn/year.
Though not a substantial sum, it would signal to investors that
Prime Minister Andy Burnham isn’t afraid to go near the thorny
issue of raising taxes. But in light of recent pension changes,
